113                                                                         46.3. The planar tables


Consequently a chain-level identiﬁcation of either planar series with an ordered bar requires a speciﬁed
transverse multiplication together with a quasi-isomorphism from its planar complex to that bar, and by
(i) and (ii) no such quasi-isomorphism exists for the algebras named.

Remark 46.6 (The type error beneath the numerical one). The bar homology of 𝑈 ( 𝐿1 ) is bigraded by
cohomological degree and weight, and is finite in each degree only because Theorem 46.3 concentrates it
in two weights. A formula assigning one integer to each degree, with no weight truncation named, does
not specify an invariant of a graded object with infinite-dimensional graded pieces.




                                                                                                     113
Chapter 47


The obstruction constitution

Each statement below names what is not determined, and therefore what must be supplied. Without
them the preceding theorems admit false corollaries.
Proposition 47.1 (Where a mixed distributive law can live). The sector on which a mixed distributive law
between ⊗! and ★ch is geometrically deﬁned is the common-decomposition sector, the image of the aligned
idempotent 𝑒al . Off that sector the two products admit no interchange, only the aligned retraction, so no
distributive law can be written.
Proof. A distributive law requires a natural transformation interchanging the two products in a fixed direction,
subject to the coherence conditions of each. The canonical comparison in the additive model is the aligned inclusion
𝜄 with retraction 𝜋 , and 𝜋 𝜄 = id while 𝜄𝜋 = 𝑒al ≠ id in general. A natural transformation subject to both coherence
conditions therefore exists only where 𝑒al acts as the identity.

    This locates a mixed law without constructing one. Its existence on this sector remains a separate ques-
tion. Zamolodchikov–Faddeev algebras provide a possible setting, since their spectral variable belongs
to the chiral direction while their word belongs to the transverse direction.
Theorem 47.2 (No quantum group from dimension loss). The dimension of the kernel of 𝑉 ⊗ 𝑛 →
Sym𝑛 𝑉 determines no 𝑅-matrix, associator, or quantum group. That kernel carries no multiplication,
coproduct, monodromy, or coherence equation.
Proof. The kernel is a representation of Σ𝑛 and nothing more. Two algebras with the same underlying graded
vector space, hence the same kernel dimensions in every arity, may have different multiplications and inequivalent
representation categories; a free algebra and a quadratic quotient with the same Hilbert series is an example. A
quantum-group structure is a coproduct together with coherence data, none of which is a function of a dimension.


Theorem 47.3 (No modular object from topology alone). The identity 𝜕 2 = 0 on M 𝑔,𝑛 assigns no
coeﬃcient operations to boundary strata. A modular master element exists only after cyclic contractions and
clutching-compatible coeﬃcient maps have been supplied.
Theorem 47.4 (No bulk from a boundary centre). The derived centre is terminal among algebraic
central actions on the boundary algebra. It does not detect a decoupled bulk sector, so a physical bulk is not
determined by the boundary chiral algebra unless a bulk-to-centre comparison is supplied and proved to be
an equivalence.
Proof. Terminality is the universal property of the centre. It is a statement about the boundary category and is
insensitive to any sector acting trivially on it, so a bulk with trivial boundary action is invisible.

Theorem 47.5 (No classification by central charge). Central charge determines the Virasoro anomaly
coeﬃcient. It does not determine the chiral operation, the representation category, the braid monodromy, or
the higher-genus conformal blocks. Distinct holomorphic theories at equal central charge exist.


                                                        115
47. The obstruction constitution                                                                                  116


Theorem 47.6 (No identification of anomalies of different types). A current-algebra level, a Virasoro
central charge, a determinant-line curvature, a BRST ghost-number anomaly, and a Borcherds-product
weight are invariants in different theories, living in different deformation complexes. They may be related
by a theorem in a particular family. They are not one universal scalar, and no identity among them follows
from numerical agreement.
Proof. Each is a class in a distinct complex: an Atiyah-algebra extension class, a Virasoro cocycle, the curvature of
a determinant line, a class in 𝐻 1 or 𝐻 2 of a BRST convolution complex, and a weight in the character lattice of an
automorphic form. A map between two of these complexes is additional data; in its absence, equality of numbers
is a coincidence of values, not an identification of classes.

Theorem 47.7 (No unnamed comparison of bars). An asserted equality Bar⊥ ( 𝐴) = Barch       Ass ( 𝐴) has
no meaning without a functor carrying the pointwise multiplication problem to the chiral-convolution
multiplication problem. The two have different underlying gradings, counting interval subdivisions and
collision trees respectively. Agreement of Euler characteristics is not a comparison map.

Theorem 47.8 (A scalar sequence is not homology data). A sequence of integers becomes bar homology only
after a chain complex, a differential, a grading convention, and a rank computation or quasi-isomorphism
are speciﬁed. Matching initial values, discriminants, or growth rates constructs no such map.
Proof. Chain-space dimensions and homology dimensions differ by the ranks of the differentials, which are
not functions of the chain dimensions. Calculation 46.2 exhibits an algebra whose bar chain dimensions are
3, 9, 27, 81, 243 and whose bar homology dimensions are 1, 0, 0, 1; Proposition 46.4 exhibits two sequences with
the same discriminant and growth rate that are transforms of one another and of neither homology.

    Theorem 47.6 together with the central-charge statement excludes treating a tuple of these scalars
as a single classifying invariant, or asserting a trace identity among them without a named comparison
morphism.

48.2    Unimodularity
Theorem 48.2 (Unimodularity of every finite jet). For every 𝑑 ≥ 2 and every 𝑥 ∈ 𝔥≤ 𝑑 ,

                                              Tr𝔥≤ 𝑑 (ad 𝑥 ) = 0.

Proof. A homogeneous Hamiltonian of degree greater than two raises polynomial degree. Its adjoint action is
                                                    L
strictly upper triangular in the decomposition 𝑟𝑑=2 𝑅𝑟 , hence has trace zero. A quadratic Hamiltonian belongs
to 𝔰𝔩 2 . Its action on each finite-dimensional 𝔰𝔩 2 -module 𝑅𝑟 has trace zero. Linearity gives the result.

Corollary 48.3 (Canonical finite BV measure). The linear odd symplectic space 𝑇 ∗ [−1]𝔥≤ 𝑑 [1] has a
translation-invariant Berezinian for which the cotangent BF Hamiltonian satisﬁes the quantum master
equation.
Proof. Choose dual linear coordinates on the shifted cotangent space and use their constant Berezinian. The
classical term vanishes by Jacobi, while the BV divergence is the adjoint trace. Unimodularity makes that trace
zero.

The universal boundary brane

50.1    Polarization
Place the cotangent theory on a half-plane. The standard Lagrangian boundary condition fixes the
cotangent field and leaves the 𝔥≤ 𝑑 field free. Boundary observables form the Rees enveloping algebra

                              𝑈ℏ (𝔥≤ 𝑑 ) = 𝑇 (𝔥≤ 𝑑 )⟦ℏ⟧/⟨ 𝑥𝑦 − 𝑦𝑥 − ℏ[ 𝑥, 𝑦 ]⟩ .

The Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt symmetrization gives a filtered vector-space isomorphism
                                                               ∼
                                    symℏ : Sym(𝔥≤ 𝑑 )⟦ℏ⟧ −
                                                         → 𝑈ℏ (𝔥≤ 𝑑 ) .

Theorem 50.1 (Boundary quantization). The factorization algebra of boundary observables of the ﬁnite
cotangent theory is locally constant and its value on an interval is 𝑈ℏ (𝔥≤ 𝑑 ). Fusion of adjacent intervals is
multiplication in the enveloping algebra.
Proof. Canonical quantization turns the linear boundary phase space 𝔥∗≤ 𝑑 into the Gutt star algebra. For a linear
Poisson bracket the Gutt product is transported by PBW to 𝑈ℏ (𝔥≤ 𝑑 ). Excision identifies ordered interval fusion
with multiplication.

of the star algebra. Tamarkin’s refinement transports the full homotopy Gerstenhaber structure.

Theorem 53.1 (Continuous formal-Darboux equivalence). A stable formality morphism can be chosen
compatibly with the quotient maps 𝔥≤ 𝑑+1 → 𝔥≤ 𝑑 . It induces a continuous homotopy-E2 quasi-isomorphism
                                                       ∼

A compatible Swiss-cheese reﬁnement gives the full formal mixed holomorphic–topological open–closed
structure of the pointed Hamiltonian BF sector. Its cohomological Gerstenhaber structure is independent of
the chosen chain-level reﬁnement.
Proof. The Taylor coefficients of a stable formality morphism are universal finite sums of graphs. In any fixed
polynomial weight, finitely many jets and graphs contribute. The maps therefore commute with the jet projections.
The finite complexes form Mittag–Leffler towers in each weight, so inverse limit preserves the finite-level quasi-
isomorphisms. Brace operations are defined by finite insertion in every weight and pass to the same limit.

Principle 53.2 (Universal brane principle). The boundary algebra 𝑈bℏ (𝔥) is the universal formal Hamil-
tonian brane. Finite matrix branes are representation-theoretic images of this algebra. The bulk is its
complete derived centre.

                             { ℎ, 𝑒 } = 2𝑒,          { ℎ, 𝑓 } = −2 𝑓 ,           { 𝑒, 𝑓 } = ℎ.

Thus 𝔥≤2 = 𝑅2 is 𝔰𝔩 2 with its standard Chevalley basis. The finite cotangent theory for 𝑑 = 2 is the
linear BV theory of 𝑇 ∗ [−1]𝔰𝔩 2 and its boundary algebra is 𝑈ℏ (𝔰𝔩 2 ).

54.2    Cubic Hamiltonians
Let
                                                   3− 𝑗 𝑗
                                          𝑣𝑗 = 𝑧1      𝑧2 ,         0 ≤ 𝑗 ≤ 3.
Then
               { ℎ, 𝑣𝑗 } = (3 − 2𝑗 ) 𝑣𝑗 ,          { 𝑒, 𝑣𝑗 } = 𝑗𝑣𝑗 −1 ,           { 𝑓 , 𝑣𝑗 } = (3 − 𝑗 ) 𝑣𝑗 +1 ,
with the boundary terms understood as zero. Hence 𝑅3 is the irreducible highest-weight-three module.

Proposition 54.1 (The cubic jet algebra). The degree-three jet algebra is the semidirect product

                                              𝔥≤3  𝔰𝔩 2 ⋉ Sym3 (ℂ2 ) ,

where the second summand is abelian.
Proof. The bracket of two cubic polynomials has degree four and vanishes in the degree-three quotient. The
bracket with a quadratic polynomial is the displayed 𝔰𝔩 2 action.

54.3    The first nonlinear layer
Write 𝑤 𝑘 = 𝑧14− 𝑘 𝑧2𝑘 , 0 ≤ 𝑘 ≤ 4. In 𝔥≤4 the cubic modes satisfy

                                            {𝑣𝑖 , 𝑣𝑗 } = 3( 𝑗 − 𝑖 ) 𝑤 𝑖 +𝑗 −1 ,

whenever the index lies between 0 and 4; the coefficient makes every remaining case vanish.

Calculation 54.2 (Two brackets).

                       { 𝑧13 , 𝑧23 } = 9 𝑧12 𝑧22 = 9𝑤2 ,        { 𝑧12 𝑧2 , 𝑧1 𝑧22 } = 3 𝑧12 𝑧22 = 3𝑤2 .

The two independent cubic pairs reach the same quartic channel with amplitudes in the ratio 3 : 1.


                                                              137
54. The first three Hamiltonian jets                                                                            138


Proof. Differentiate each monomial in the two symplectic coordinates and substitute in the Poisson formula. The
first pair gives 3 𝑧12 · 3 𝑧22 . The second gives 2 𝑧1 𝑧2 · 2 𝑧1 𝑧2 − 𝑧12 · 𝑧22 = 3 𝑧12 𝑧22 .

Theorem 54.3 (The quartic jet presentation). The Lie algebra 𝔥≤4 is generated by 𝑒, 𝑓 , ℎ, 𝑣0 , 𝑣1 , 𝑣2 , 𝑣3 .
Its relations are the 𝔰𝔩 2 relations, the irreducible action on the 𝑣𝑗 , formula (54.3), and the induced 𝔰𝔩 2 action
on 𝑅4 . The quartic subspace is abelian in the quotient.
Proof. Quadratic and cubic generators produce every quartic monomial through (54.3). Brackets involving a
quartic and a cubic have degree five, and brackets of two quartics have degree six, so both vanish after projection.
Jacobi follows from the polynomial Poisson bracket before projection.

55.1   The bidifferential formula
For polynomials on ℂ2 , the Moyal product is

                              ∑︁ 1 ℏ 𝑛 ∑︁

                              𝑛 ≥0 𝑛 !  2  𝑟 =0        𝑟

Every polynomial pair gives a finite sum.

Theorem 55.1 (Quadratic exactness). For polynomials 𝑓 , 𝑔 of degree at most two,

                                                [ 𝑓 , 𝑔 ] ★ = ℏ{ 𝑓 , 𝑔 } .

Thus the quadratic Moyal algebra is the Rees enveloping algebra of 𝔰𝔩 2 together with the Heisenberg trans-
lations when linear Hamiltonians are retained.
Proof. The commutator retains the odd powers of the Poisson bidifferential. Derivatives of order three vanish on
quadratics, so the first-order term is the entire commutator.

                                                                    3

Proof. In (55.1), the odd orders are 𝑛 = 1 and 𝑛 = 3. The first contributes 9ℏ 𝑧12 𝑧22 . The third contributes
2(ℏ/2) 3 (3!) = 3ℏ3 /2.

55.2    Weyl generators
Let 𝑞 act by multiplication and let 𝑝 = ℏ 𝜕𝑞 . Then

                                                     [ 𝑝, 𝑞 ] = ℏ.

Weyl ordering identifies polynomial symbols with differential operators. The Moyal formula is the
pullback of operator composition under this identification.

Chapter 58


All-arity open–closed operations

58.1    Hochschild braces
For Hochschild cochains 𝐹 ∈ 𝐶 𝑝 ( 𝐴, 𝐴) and 𝐺𝑖 ∈ 𝐶 𝑞𝑖 ( 𝐴, 𝐴), the brace

                                                  𝐹 {𝐺1 , . . . , 𝐺𝑟 }

is the signed sum over ordered insertions of the 𝐺𝑖 into the inputs of 𝐹 . The brace identities state that a
nested insertion is the sum over all ways of distributing the inner insertions among the outer slots.

Theorem 58.1 (Brace realization of the universal centre). For 𝐴 = 𝑈ℏ (𝔥≤ 𝑑 ), choose the Tamarkin–
Kontsevich homotopy-Gerstenhaber reﬁnement of Φ𝑑 . It transports the homotopy Schouten operations of
polyvector ﬁelds to the Hochschild brace algebra. The binary operation is the Gerstenhaber bracket, and the
mixed operation with one boundary word is the quantized Hamiltonian action.
Proof. Kontsevich formality is an algebra over a cofibrant resolution of the Gerstenhaber operad. Tamarkin’s
formality identifies that resolution with the brace operad. Twisting by the linear Poisson Maurer–Cartan element
produces the differential and preserves every brace operation.

58.2    The first mixed identities
Let 𝐹 be a closed cochain and 𝑎, 𝑏 boundary operators. The first Swiss-cheese identities are

                                        𝐹 · ( 𝑎𝑏 ) = ( 𝐹(1) · 𝑎) ( 𝐹(2) · 𝑏 ) ,

for the coproduct encoded by the brace diagonal, and

                             [ 𝐹 , 𝐺 ] · 𝑎 = 𝐹 · ( 𝐺 · 𝑎) − (−1) | 𝐹 | | 𝐺 | 𝐺 · ( 𝐹 · 𝑎) .

For linear Hamiltonians these are the Leibniz rule and the commutator identity of derivations. Higher
braces provide their coherent homotopies.

Corollary 58.2 (All-arity formal-Darboux theorem). The inverse limit Φ∞ equips the pair
                                                               
                                        ∗
                                       𝐶CE (𝑇 ∗ [−1]𝔥) , 𝑈
                                                         bℏ (𝔥)

with a complete homotopy Swiss-cheese algebra. Its closed color is the full derived centre of the universal
boundary algebra.
Proof. Every fixed polynomial weight receives finitely many brace insertions, so the finite-level Swiss-cheese oper-
ations commute with the jet projections. Their separated inverse limit gives the complete action. Morita theory
identifies Hochschild cochains of the boundary algebra with its derived centre.

Chapter 59


Finite Chan–Paton ranks

59.1    Rank one
For 𝑁 = 1, the commuting equation is automatic. The boundary coordinate algebra is ℂ[ 𝑥, 𝑦 ], and

                                                   𝐽1 ( 𝑓 ) = 𝑓 ( 𝑥, 𝑦 ) .

The necklace bracket reduces to the ordinary Poisson bracket. Restriction to 𝑓 ∈ 𝔪2 gives the defining
pointed-Hamiltonian representation. The two linear Hamiltonians act by translations and recover the
affine center-of-mass extension.

59.2     Rank two and Cayley–Hamilton reduction
For a 2 × 2 matrix 𝑋 ,
                                         𝑋 2 − (Tr 𝑋 ) 𝑋 + (det 𝑋 ) 𝐼 = 0.
Hence every trace containing two consecutive powers of 𝑋 reduces to traces with 𝑋 -degree at most one
after adjoining Tr 𝑋 and det 𝑋 . The same statement holds for 𝑌 . On the commuting locus, simultaneous
diagonalization identifies the invariant algebra with the symmetric polynomials in two ordered pairs.

Proposition 59.1 (First finite-rank trace relation). For commuting 2 × 2 matrices,
                                        2                                2
                                 𝑝20 = 𝑝10 − 2 det 𝑋 ,            𝑝02 = 𝑝01 − 2 det 𝑌 ,

and
                                                                         2
                                         𝑝112 − 𝑝20 𝑝02 = − 𝑥1 𝑦2 − 𝑥2 𝑦1 .
The stable generators remain independent until a relation forced by the rank is reached.
Proof. Write the joint eigenvalues as ( 𝑥1 , 𝑦1 ) and ( 𝑥2 , 𝑦2 ) and expand both sides.

59.3    Stable passage
The transition from rank 𝑁 to rank 𝑁 + 1 adds a zero eigenvalue. Each fixed weighted degree stabilizes
once 𝑁 exceeds that degree. The inverse limit is therefore the completed multisymmetric algebra. The
stable trace is an exact quotient of the universal centre on the polynomial trace sector.

Fedosov descent

60.1    The Weyl algebroid
Let ( 𝑆, 𝜔) be a holomorphic symplectic surface. The bundle of formal Weyl algebras has fibre the
completed Weyl algebra of a formal symplectic plane. A holomorphic symplectic connection and a
Fedosov one-form define a flat connection
                                                       1
                                              𝐷 𝐹 = ∇ + [ 𝛾, −]
                                                       ℏ
whose flat sections form a deformation-quantization algebroid W𝑆,ℏ .

Theorem 60.1 (Globalized formality on a holomorphic symplectic surface). Fix a stable formality
morphism and a compatible homotopy-E2 reﬁnement. There is a sheafwise homotopy-E2 quasi-isomorphism
                                                              ∼
                             Φ 𝑆 : (PV 𝑆 ⟦ℏ⟧, [ℏ 𝜋, −]) −  •
                                                        → 𝐶HH (W𝑆,ℏ , W𝑆,ℏ ) ,

where 𝜋 = 𝜔 −1 . A change of holomorphic symplectic connection or Fedosov one-form gives a gauge-equivalent
resolution and a homotopic quasi-isomorphism. Holomorphic symplectomorphisms act equivariantly on
the resulting homotopy class.
Proof. Fedosov resolutions replace both sheaves by acyclic sheaves of formal fibrewise polyvectors and polydifferen-
tial operators. The fixed covariant formality morphism acts fibrewise and intertwines the Fedosov differentials. A
change of connection or Fedosov one-form is a gauge transformation in the pronilpotent resolution and therefore
gives a homotopy.

Corollary 60.2 (Global mixed-HT centre). The deformation-quantization category Perf (W𝑆,ℏ ) has
Morita centre
                        𝑍 Mor Perf (W𝑆,ℏ ) ≃ ℝΓ( 𝑆, PV 𝑆 ⟦ℏ⟧, [ℏ 𝜋, −]) .
                                          

For a K3 surface this gives a global open–closed equivalence for the Hamiltonian BF sector.
Proof. The Morita centre of perfect modules over an algebroid is the derived global section complex of its
Hochschild cochains. Apply the globalized formality equivalence and then derived global sections. A K3 surface is
holomorphic symplectic, so the stated Hamiltonian sector is the resulting Poisson-BV complex.

Factorization and descent

61.1   The algebraic mixed-HT realization
The sheaf
                                           P𝑆 = (PV 𝑆 ⟦ℏ⟧, [ℏ 𝜋, −])
is a sheaf of complete homotopy-E2 algebras. Dunn additivity gives its locally constant factorization
realization in two oriented topological directions. The holomorphic sheaf variable retains the dependence
on 𝑆 .

Theorem 61.1 (Algebraic factorization realization). The homotopy-E2 sheaf P𝑆 determines a complete
mixed holomorphic–topological factorization algebra
                                             alg
                                           A 𝑆,bulk   on    ℝ2top × 𝑆.

Its universal boundary category is Perf (W𝑆,ℏ ), and the bulk–boundary morphism is a local equivalence
                                        alg    ∼   der             
                                      A 𝑆,bulk −
                                               → 𝑍 Mor Perf (W𝑆,ℏ ) .

On every formal Darboux chart the map is the completed Kontsevich–Duﬂo equivalence.
Proof. The factorization envelope of a complete homotopy-E2 algebra is locally constant on oriented two-disks.
Sheafwise application to P𝑆 gives the first assertion. The Fedosov algebroid is the boundary quantization of the
same linear Poisson data. Globalized formality identifies its Hochschild cochains with P𝑆 , and Morita invariance
identifies Hochschild cochains with the centre of the perfect-module category. The construction is local on 𝑆 and
therefore respects factorization descent.

61.2    BV realization
The cyclic Lie sheaf of formal Hamiltonians has the shifted-cotangent action
                                           ∫       D                        E

Its classical observables map to A 𝑆,bulk by the Chevalley–Poisson identification. A compatible gauge

The finite jet zero modes carry the exact quantum measure constructed above.

The harmonic square

The four complexes
             ∗

are connected by explicit maps. The first two are equivalent by formality. The stable trace map reaches

Their common input is the cyclic Hamiltonian class, and each construction retains a different amount

Theorem 68.1 (Four realizations of cyclic primitives). For a unital algebra 𝐵 and a Hamiltonian Lie
map 𝔥 → 𝐻 𝐶0 ( 𝐵 ), the following maps are functorial:

                                    d 𝐻 𝐶0 ( 𝐵 ) −→ O (Rep 𝐵 ) 𝐺 𝐿∞ ,

The ﬁrst map preserves the homotopy Gerstenhaber structure, the second preserves the necklace Poisson bracket,
the third preserves multiplication, and the fourth preserves the Hopf structure.
Proof. The first arrow is the inverse limit of the finite-jet Kontsevich–Duflo formality maps. The graph formulas
preserve braces and the Schouten bracket up to their canonical coherent homotopies, and compatibility with jet
quotients passes this structure to the complete limit.
   A cyclic class [ 𝑏 ] ∈ 𝐻 𝐶0 ( 𝐵 ) = 𝐵 /[ 𝐵, 𝐵 ] defines the invariant function

                                            Tr 𝑁 ( [ 𝑏 ]) ( 𝜌) = Tr( 𝜌 ( 𝑏 ))

on the representation scheme. Cyclicity makes this function independent of the representative of [ 𝑏 ]. The double-
Poisson or Hamiltonian hypothesis on 𝔥 → 𝐻 𝐶0 ( 𝐵 ) converts the necklace bracket into the Poisson bracket of
trace functions, which proves the second assertion.
    Products of single traces define compatible multisymmetric functions as 𝑁 grows. The universal property of
the completed symmetric algebra therefore gives the third map. Its multiplicativity is built into the passage from a
cyclic primitive to its multi-trace exponential assembly.
    The stable Lie algebra 𝔤𝔩 ∞ 𝐵 has block-sum coproduct. The Loday–Quillen–Tsygan theorem identifies its
primitive homology with 𝐻 𝐶∗−1 ( 𝐵 ) and identifies the full stable homology with the free graded-commutative
Hopf algebra on those primitives. This gives the fourth isomorphism and its Hopf compatibility. Naturality of
formality, trace evaluation, stabilization, and the Loday–Quillen–Tsygan map proves functoriality of the four
constructions.

Chapter 69


Synthesis

The finite jets supply exact BV theories. Their boundary quantizations supply universal branes. Linear
Poisson formality identifies bulk and centre. Universal graph formulas pass to the formal limit. Fedosov
descent globalizes the equivalence. Matrix substitution produces finite Chan–Paton branes, necklace
Hamiltonians, and stable traces. The Duflo wheels measure the quantum difference between symmetric
and enveloping coordinates.
   The complete chain is

                               ∗ (𝑇 ∗ [−1]𝔥)      Φ∞      ∗ (𝑈
                                                             bℏ 𝔥, 𝑈
                                                                   bℏ 𝔥)
                              𝐶CE                  ∼     𝐶HH

                          representation                          module action

                                                               ∗ ( 𝐴 , 𝐴 ).
                              O (Rep 𝑁 ) 𝐺 𝐿 𝑁 central action 𝐶HH   𝑁   𝑁


The upper arrow is the universal open–closed equivalence. The lower row is its finite-brane image. This
order retains the centre before taking traces and retains the field theory before taking its character.

Bibliographic note

The finite and global formality maps used above are the linear and covariant forms of Kontsevich formality
and its Fedosov globalization [14, 28, 52]. The Hochschild model of enveloping algebras is the Cartan–
Eilenberg comparison. The all-arity centre action is the Deligne–Tamarkin brace action [53, 60]. The
global deformation-quantization category is the holomorphic Weyl algebroid. The matrix trace and
necklace formulas are the noncommutative symplectic moment-map construction.

Calabi–Yau categories

73.1   Smoothness, properness, and the Calabi–Yau class
Let C be a small stable 𝕜-linear category. Smoothness means that the diagonal bimodule is perfect.
Properness means that the morphism complexes are perfect over 𝕜. A 𝑑 -Calabi–Yau structure is a
nondegenerate negative-cyclic class
                                          𝜃 CY ∈ 𝐻 𝐶𝑑− (C)
whose underlying Hochschild trace identifies the diagonal bimodule with its dual shifted by 𝑑 .
  For 𝐸 ∈ C, the tangent complex of the derived moduli stack of objects is

                                       𝑇𝐸 M C ≃ RHom C ( 𝐸, 𝐸 ) [1] .

The Calabi–Yau trace pairs two tangent vectors by composition and trace.

Theorem 73.1 (The two universal categorical outputs). Let C be a smooth proper 𝑑 -Calabi–Yau category.
   (i) The Hochschild cochains 𝐶𝐶 ∗ (C) carry a framed E2 -algebra structure.
  (ii) Whenever the moduli stack of objects is representable in the PTVV setting, M C carries a (2 − 𝑑 )-
       shifted symplectic form.
Proof. The cyclic Deligne theorem upgrades the brace E2 structure on Hochschild cochains by the circle action
supplied by the cyclic trace [11]. The shifted form at 𝐸 is

                                                ◦                       Tr
                          RHom( 𝐸, 𝐸 ) [1] ⊗2 −−→ RHom( 𝐸, 𝐸 ) [2] −−→ 𝕜 [2 − 𝑑 ] .

Nondegeneracy is the Calabi–Yau duality isomorphism, and closedness is the negative-cyclic lift [10, 65].

  The integer 𝑑 controls the shift of the moduli form. The Hochschild operation remains framed E2 .
These structures meet through deformation theory while retaining their separate operadic degrees.




                                                      187
Chapter 74


Local BV theories and factorization observables

74.1    Cyclic elliptic local Lie algebras
Let 𝑋 be a complex manifold. A classical local field theory is specified by a sheaf L of elliptic 𝐿∞ algebras,
a local invariant pairing, and an action functional 𝑆 satisfying the classical master equation. For an open
set 𝑈 ⊂ 𝑋 , the classical observables are
                                                      ∗
                                        Obscl (𝑈 ) = 𝐶CE (L 𝑐 (𝑈 )) .

Disjoint union of supports gives the prefactorization product.

Theorem 74.1 (Factorization observables). An elliptic classical BV theory produces a factorization algebra
Obscl . A renormalized quantization satisfying the quantum master equation produces a factorization
algebra Obsq over 𝕜 [[ℏ]].
Proof. The Chevalley cochains of a direct sum are the completed tensor product of the cochains, giving the
disjoint-support multiplication. Elliptic descent supplies the Weiss cosheaf property. In the quantum theory,
renormalized time-ordered products define the structure maps, and the quantum master equation makes their
differential square-zero and compatible with nested ultraviolet contractions [19, 20].

74.2    Boundary and defect restriction
Let 𝑌 = 𝐶 × ℝ ≥0 , with 𝐶 a complex curve, and let a boundary condition be given at 𝐶 × {0}. Boundary
observables are holomorphic along 𝐶 and ordered along the inward normal line. A compact boundary
object 𝑏 in the boundary factorization category gives

                                            𝐴𝑏 = RHom( 𝑏, 𝑏 ) .

Theorem 74.2 (Ordered boundary algebra). When boundary observables are holomorphic on 𝐶 and
locally constant along the oriented normal direction, 𝐴𝑏 is an ordered chiral algebra
                                                                   
                                        𝐴𝑏 ∈ AlgE1 Fact𝐷 ( 𝐶 ) , ⊗! .

If 𝑏 is a local compact generator and the Morita equivalences obey factorization descent, the boundary
category is equivalent to 𝐴𝑏 -modules in factorization coeﬃcients.
Proof. Composition of boundary endomorphisms gives the internal E1 multiplication. Holomorphic collision
gives the factorization coefficient on 𝐶 . The componentwise locality identity makes multiplication compatible
with every disjoint-support factorization map. Local compact-generator Morita theory descends over the Ran
diagrams. These are the recognition and Morita theorems of Volume I.

Chapter 75


Three curve-level algebraic outputs

Let 𝐶 be a smooth curve and let D𝐶 be the fixed Ran category. The same geometric source can produce
three algebraic outputs, and their distinction is functorial.

Definition 75.1 (Curve-level faces). A transverse face is

                                         𝐴⊥ ∈ AlgE1 (Fact𝐷 ( 𝐶 ) , ⊗! ) .

An associative chiral face is
                                              𝐴ch                 ch
                                               Ass ∈ AlgE1 (D𝐶 , ★ ) .

A chiral Lie face is
                                              𝔤ch ∈ AlgLie (D𝐶 , ★ch ) .
Their bars are Bar⊥ , Barch           ch
                         Ass , and BarLie .

Theorem 75.2 (Source separation). A boundary normal direction determines the transverse face. A
chiral-convolution multiplication determines the associative chiral face. A Lie bracket for chiral convolution
determines the Lie face. The commutator functor sends the associative chiral face to the Lie face. A chiral
enveloping algebra 𝑈 ch (𝔤) satisﬁes

                                         Barch    ch         ch
                                            Ass (𝑈 (𝔤)) ≃ BarLie (𝔤)

on the nilcomplete PBW locus. A mixed source joins the transverse and associative chiral faces through an
aligned distributive law.
Proof. Each statement is the corresponding operadic restriction or adjunction in the named monoidal category.
The PBW equivalence is the enveloping lane of chiral Koszul duality. The mixed statement is the distributive-law
theorem of Volume I.

    This theorem replaces every dimension-based dispatch of an E𝑛 level. The geometry of the source
selects the available directions; the operadic level records the operations actually constructed.

Pushforward to a curve

Let 𝑝 : 𝑋 → 𝐶 be a holomorphic map to a complex curve. For a factorization algebra 𝔽 on 𝑋 , define

                                           ( 𝑝∗ 𝔽 ) (𝑈 ) = 𝔽 ( 𝑝 −1𝑈 ) .

Theorem 77.1 (Factorization pushforward). The assignment 𝑝∗ 𝔽 is a factorization algebra on 𝐶 . Suppose
in addition that 𝑝 is proper and locally trivial in a topology compatible with the factorization theory, that
𝔽 is locally constant in the ﬁber directions, and that product-chart excision and base change hold. Then on
every trivializing open 𝑈 ⊂ 𝐶 with ﬁber 𝐹𝑝 there is a natural equivalence
                                                          ∫
                                          ( 𝑝∗ 𝔽 ) (𝑈 ) ≃   𝔽 | 𝐹𝑝 ×𝑈 ,
                                                             𝐹𝑝

compatible on overlaps. If the resulting object is holomorphic and regular holonomic along 𝐶 , Riemann–
Hilbert algebraization gives a factorization 𝐷 -module on 𝐶 .
Proof. Disjoint opens in 𝐶 have disjoint inverse images, so the factorization products push forward. Weiss descent
is inherited from inverse images of Weiss covers. On a product chart, local constancy in the fiber and factoriza-
tion excision identify the value with fiberwise factorization homology. The base-change hypothesis glues these
local equivalences. Regular-holonomic Riemann–Hilbert transports the holomorphic factorization object to the
algebraic 𝐷 -module setting.

  Thus a curve shadow is attached to an actual map and an actual field theory. Different fibrations and
boundary conditions produce different ordered chiral algebras from the same categorical source.

The derived centre and the physical bulk

For an ordered boundary algebra 𝐴, define

                                    𝑍 ord ( 𝐴) = RHom 𝐴⊗ 𝐴op ( 𝐴, 𝐴) .

It is a canonical E2 algebra. A bulk theory acting on the boundary gives

                                      𝛽 : Obsbulk | 𝜕 −→ 𝑍 ord ( 𝐴) .

Definition 78.1 (Open–closed completeness). A boundary condition is open–closed complete on a
protected sector when 𝛽 is an equivalence on that sector.

   The definition turns the phrase ‘‘bulk equals centre’’ into a comparison theorem. The centre exists
algebraically for every ordered boundary algebra; the physical bulk is identified with it by the open–closed
map.

                                                    𝑝          𝑞

   For rational constructible complexes 𝐾𝛾 , a coefficient datum includes actual degree-zero morphisms

                                    𝑐 𝛼,𝛽 : 𝑅𝑞∗ 𝑝∗ ( 𝐾𝛼 ⊠ 𝐾𝛽 ) −→ 𝐾𝛼+ 𝛽 ,

with all shifts included in the complexes. The two coefficient composites on the filtration stack must

additionally requires convergence in each output degree.

Theorem 79.1 (Hall associativity). Assume the coeﬃcient maps 𝑐 𝛼,𝛽 , the properness and base-change
hypotheses, and the identity 𝐿 = 𝑅 for every two-step ﬁltration as in Theorem 37.1. Then

is an associative product on the indicated rational hypercohomology.
Proof. Both parenthesizations pull the three input classes to the stack of filtrations 0 ⊂ 𝐸1 ⊂ 𝐸2 ⊂ 𝐸3 . Proper
base change identifies their coefficient complexes with 𝐷 in Theorem 37.1. They apply 𝐿 and 𝑅 to this same class.
The assumed equality of these derived maps gives associativity.

    Chapter 133 constructs the rational coefficient maps for the three-loop potential Tr( 𝐴 [ 𝐵, 𝐶 ]) in
block sizes (1, 1), (2, 1), (3, 1) and (2, 2). Chapter 134 constructs (4, 1) and (2, 3). Theorems 133.4 and 134.1
identify the two composites with a single flag map for the ordered triples (2, 1, 1) and (2, 2, 1), respectively.
The (2, 2) product retains both nonzero rank-two potentials. In the second triple the intermediate
potentials are 𝑓4 + 0 and 𝑓2 + 𝑓3 , where 𝑓𝑛 ( 𝐴, 𝐵, 𝐶 ) = Tr( 𝐴 [ 𝐵, 𝐶 ]) on Mat𝑛 (ℂ) 3 . The support product
of vanishing cycles is used as a morphism; an isomorphism for the pair ( 𝑓2 , 𝑓3 ) is not assumed.
    More explicitly, on [Crit( 𝑓𝑛 )/GL𝑛 ] let P𝑛 be the restriction of the equivariant complex Φ 𝑓𝑛 ℚ[2𝑛2 ],
with Φ 𝑓 = Cone( 𝑖 ∗ → 𝜓𝑓 ) [−1], where 𝑖 includes the zero fibre and 𝜓𝑓 is nearby cycles. The ambient
incidence variety for a preserved two-plane inside a four-plane in ℂ5 has complex dimension 59, and the
matrix space has dimension 75. Its embedding in the product of the matrix space with the flag variety has
complex normal rank 75 + 8 − 59 = 24, giving Thom shift 48; integration over Fl(2, 4; 5) of dimension

79. Extension correspondences                                                                                202


8 gives net shift 32. Thus the input normalization 8 + 8 + 2 = 18 reaches the output normalization 50.
The two factorizations have shifts 16 + 16 and 8 + 24. Proper exchange for Φ, naturality of its support
maps and the complex Thom orientations identify the actual derived morphisms. Equivariance retains
stabilizers, and these maps commute with potential monodromy. The cyclic fivefold collision gives a
thirty-sheet trace with six ramified branches and nontrivial nearby-cycle gluing. Its central matrix is a
regular nilpotent Jordan block. The coefficient images at scalar matrices in ranks three, four and five
remain separate calculations.
    The regular traces use the generators 𝑒𝑛 of Proposition 133.5. Relative to the cyclic 𝐵 -polarized gener-

the (2, 2, 1) trace is +30 in both. These are coordinates of the same coefficient maps. The separated

Consequently the proved submodule-first triples (2, 1, 1) and (2, 2, 1) give the quotient-first triples (1, 1, 2)
and (1, 2, 2). These identities supply the corresponding components of 𝐿 = 𝑅. Identities for other triples
and higher coherence remain additional constructions. Neither a coproduct nor a compact-threefold
comparison follows from these two filtration identities.

Chapter 80


Orientation data and the threefold Hall
product

For compact Calabi–Yau threefolds, natural orientation data on moduli of coherent sheaves and perfect
complexes is constructed by Joyce and Upmeier [48]. A general cohomological Hall algebra for a 3-Calabi–
Yau category uses strong orientation data, which carries the coherent multiplicativity required by the
attractor and extension correspondences [51].

Theorem 80.1 (Threefold Hall algebra from strong orientation). Let C be a 3-Calabi–Yau category
satisfying the geometric hypotheses of Kinjo–Park–Safronov, and assume strong orientation data. Then its
cohomological Donaldson–Thomas complexes carry an associative cohomological Hall product. The product
is the parabolic-induction correspondence supplied by the attractor Lagrangian correspondence.
Proof. Kinjo–Park–Safronov prove the functoriality of the Donaldson–Thomas perverse sheaf under the attractor
Lagrangian correspondence and use strong orientation to define the parabolic-induction morphism. Their base-
change and associativity theorem gives the CoHA [51].

Definition 80.2 (Reduced 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 Hall datum). A reduced 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 Hall datum is a strong-oriented
threefold Hall datum together with descent along the elliptic translation action, a reduced deformation
theory, and a separated completion in physical charge. A full quantum-group datum additionally includes
a coproduct, Cartan extension, negative half, and nondegenerate continuous Hopf pairing.

Chapter 81


The geometric 𝐸 -equivariant Hall source

Let 𝑆 be a projective K3 surface, let 𝐸 be an elliptic curve, and put

                                                   𝑋 = 𝑆 × 𝐸.

Write Coh ≤1 ( 𝑋 ) for coherent sheaves whose support has dimension at most one. This is an extension-
closed abelian category. Its numerical charge group is denoted Γ𝑋 , and the degree of the projected
one-cycle on 𝐸 is the additive function

                                               𝑚 : Γ𝑋 −→ ℤ ≥0 .

For 𝛾 ∈ Γ𝑋 , let M 𝛾 be the moduli stack of objects of charge 𝛾 . Translation on the elliptic factor gives
an action of 𝐸 on every M 𝛾 and on every extension stack.
    Oberdieck and Shen construct the 𝐸 -equivariant Grothendieck ring of stacks and its Hall convolution.
In the present notation the completed algebra is
                                                 M
                                       H𝑋𝐸,mot = c 𝛾 ∈ Γ 𝐾0𝐸 (St/M 𝛾 ) ,
                                                           𝑋


where the completion is taken in effective curve class and Euler characteristic.

Theorem 81.1 (Geometric reduced Hall algebra). The extension correspondence in Coh ≤1 ( 𝑋 ) deﬁnes
an associative 𝐸 -equivariant motivic Hall product on H𝑋𝐸,mot . The Oberdieck–Shen reduced integration
morphism
                                  𝐼𝑋𝐸 : H𝑋𝐸,mot −→ ℚ[ \Γ𝑋 ] [ 𝜖 ]/( 𝜖 2 )
is multiplicative on the regular subalgebra on which their integration theorem is deﬁned. The coeﬃcient of
𝜖 is the reduced contribution of the positive-dimensional 𝐸 -orbits.
Proof. The stack of short exact sequences is 𝐸 -equivariant because translation preserves exactness and numerical
charge. The stack of two-step filtrations identifies the two composites of three Hall products, so equivariant base
change proves associativity. The integration statement is the 𝐸 -equivariant motivic Hall theorem of Oberdieck–
Shen [64]. Their stabilizer stratification separates fixed contributions from moving elliptic orbits and packages the
latter in the square-zero parameter 𝜖 .

Local and wrapped geometry inside the Hall
algebra

Define the completed degree-zero and positive-degree pieces
                         M                                           M
                 H𝑋loc = c 𝑚 ( 𝛾 )=0 𝐾0𝐸 (St/M 𝛾 ) ,          H𝑋wr = c 𝑚 ( 𝛾 ) >0 𝐾0𝐸 (St/M 𝛾 ) .

A pure one-dimensional support of degree zero projects to a finite subset of 𝐸 . A support of positive
degree projects onto 𝐸 . The first sector is point-local on the elliptic curve; the second is wrapped.

Theorem 82.1 (Geometric local–wrapped decomposition). Hall multiplication respects elliptic degree:

                                            H𝑚1 ∗ H𝑚2 ⊆ H𝑚1 +𝑚2 .

Consequently H𝑋loc is a subalgebra, H𝑋wr is a closed two-sided ideal, and

                                            H𝑋𝐸,mot  H𝑋loc ⋉ H𝑋wr

as a complete vector space with multiplication determined by the Hall correspondences. The reduced inte-
gration morphism preserves this decomposition.
Proof. The cycle of the middle term of a short exact sequence is the sum of the cycles of its subobject and quotient.
Projection to 𝐸 therefore adds degrees. Degree zero is closed under addition, and positive degree absorbs addition
by a nonnegative degree. The same grading is present on the target group algebra of 𝐼𝑋𝐸 .

   This theorem supplies the geometric version of the local–wrapped splitting constructed from the
Borcherds root algebra in the companion volume. The two constructions have the same additive elliptic
degree and become comparable after a primitive root framing is chosen.

Cohomological refinement and the Gram
character

A cohomological refinement uses the Donaldson–Thomas sheaf on the derived moduli stack. The datum
required for the convolution is the strong orientation coherence of Kinjo–Park–Safronov. A strong
orientation enhancement of the natural Joyce–Upmeier orientation therefore produces

                                   H𝑋𝐸,coh = c 𝛾 ∈ Γ 𝐻∗BM,𝐸 (M 𝛾 , Φ 𝛾 ) ,
                                             M
                                                          𝑋


with its cohomological Hall product.

Theorem 83.1 (Strong-oriented cohomological lift). For every strong orientation enhancement on the
𝐸 -equivariant derived moduli problem, the attractor correspondence constructs an associative cohomological
Hall algebra H𝑋𝐸,coh . Elliptic degree again gives a subalgebra in degree zero and a closed ideal in positive
degree. Forgetting the cohomological coeﬃcient system recovers the corresponding motivic Hall correspon-
dences.
Proof. The parabolic-induction theorem of Kinjo–Park–Safronov gives the cohomological product from the
strong-oriented attractor Lagrangian correspondence [51]. Numerical charge and elliptic degree are constant on
connected components of every correspondence, so the proof of the preceding decomposition applies verbatim.

Theorem 83.2 (Exact geometric character map). The map 𝐼𝑋𝐸,Π is multiplicative. Its logarithm is primi-
tive for the completed Hall coproduct whenever that coproduct is deﬁned in the selected localization. In every

reduced invariants of 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 . The primitive reduced Gromov–Witten theory supplies the independent
automorphic character − 𝜒10−1 in classes primitive in the K3 factor.

83. Cohomological refinement and the Gram character                                                            210


Proof. Multiplicativity of 𝐼𝑋𝐸 is the reduced integration theorem. The cocycle identity for the polarization cross-
term makes the graph of Π multiplicative. The logarithm of a group-like series is primitive. The enumerative
identifications are the reduced Hall calculations of Oberdieck–Shen and the primitive Igusa theorem of Oberdieck–
Pixton [63, 64].

   The geometric Hall source and the universal Igusa avatar now occupy explicit objects. A primitive
root framing is a morphism from the simple BPS sheaves of H𝑋𝐸,coh to the simple-root spaces of 𝔤Δ5
that preserves the Euler pairing, parity, and extension brackets. The universal presentation theorem then
extends this framing through Hall, current, quantum-double, and determinant constructions.

Chapter 84


BPS primitives

A connected graded bialgebra has primitive Lie algebra

                                 Prim( 𝐻 ) = { 𝑥 : Δ𝑥 = 𝑥 ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ 𝑥 } .

In cohomological Donaldson–Thomas theory, the BPS sheaf isolates the primitive part of the Hall
algebra.

Theorem 84.1 (Two-Calabi–Yau BPS algebra). For the geometric two-Calabi–Yau categories covered by
the BPS-algebra theorem, the BPS algebra is the positive enveloping algebra of a generalized Kac–Moody
Lie algebra. This scope includes semistable sheaves on K3 and other symplectic surfaces under the stated
good-moduli hypotheses.
Proof. The theorem is the Davison–Hennecart–Schlegel Mejia construction [24]. The simple BPS sheaves give
the simple-root spaces, extension correspondences give the bracket, and the integrality/PBW theorem identifies
the Hall algebra with the positive enveloping algebra.

   This result supplies a global K3 generalized Kac–Moody algebra at the two-Calabi–Yau Hall level.
Yangian structures arise from additional equivariant correspondences and coproducts in the surface and
quiver models described below.

Chapter 85


The integral Hall–Borcherds amalgam

Let 𝔭+𝑋 be a complete charge-graded BPS Lie superalgebra supplied by a geometric Hall source, and
let 𝔫Δ5 ,+ be the positive Igusa Borcherds Lie superalgebra. The polarized charge extension places both
gradings over one abelian Cartan algebra 𝔥Π . Form the two complete Borels

                                   𝔟 𝑋 = 𝔥Π ⋉ 𝔭+𝑋 ,            𝔟Δ = 𝔥Π ⋉ 𝔫Δ5 ,+ .

The Cartan acts through the physical charge on the first Borel and through Gram degree on the second.

Definition 85.1 (Integral protected Lie algebra). The integral Hall–Borcherds algebra is the completed
amalgamated coproduct
                                           𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ = 𝔟 𝑋 ⨿
                                                        b 𝔥 𝔟Δ
                                                           Π

in complete charge-filtered dg Lie superalgebras.

Theorem 85.2 (Hall–Borcherds amalgamation). The completed amalgam exists and represents pairs
of continuous Lie morphisms from 𝔟 𝑋 and 𝔟Δ that agree on 𝔥Π . The Cartan projections of the two Borels
induce canonical retractions

                                𝑟 𝑋 : 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ −→ 𝔟 𝑋 ,           𝑟Δ : 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ −→ 𝔟Δ .

Its completed enveloping algebra is the amalgamated free product

                                     b (𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ )  𝑈
                                     𝑈             b (𝔟 𝑋 )b
                                                           ∗𝑈b (𝔥Π ) 𝑈
                                                                     b (𝔟Δ ) .

The geometric Hall operations survive under 𝑟 𝑋 , while the Igusa bracket, PBW character, and automorphic
determinant survive under 𝑟Δ .
Proof. Construct the ordinary Lie coproduct from the free product of the two Borels and divide by the ideal
identifying their two Cartan copies. Complete by total positive charge and proper root height. Every fixed filtered
degree uses finitely many Lie words, so the completion is separated and has the stated mapping property. The
projection 𝔟 𝑋 → 𝔥Π → 𝔟Δ together with the identity on 𝔟Δ gives 𝑟Δ by universality; the second retraction
is symmetric. The enveloping functor preserves coproducts of Lie algebras, and PBW identifies the completed
algebraic filtration with the displayed completed free product.

Definition 85.3 (Comparison ideal). A primitive comparison 𝜑 : 𝔫Δ5 ,+ → 𝔭+𝑋 preserving Cartan degree
defines the closed ideal

                              J𝜑 = ⟨ 𝑖Δ ( 𝑥 ) − 𝑖 𝑋 ( 𝜑 ( 𝑥 )) | 𝑥 ∈ 𝔫Δ5 ,+ ⟩ ⊂ 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ .

The diagonal realization is
                                              𝔟 𝑋 ≃Δ,𝜑 = 𝔟 𝑋 ⊠Δ /J𝜑 .


                                                         213
85. The integral Hall–Borcherds amalgam                                                                         214


Theorem 85.4 (Universal comparison quotient). A Cartan-preserving primitive comparison is equivalent
to a quotient of the integral amalgam that identiﬁes the Borcherds generators with geometric BPS primitives.
If 𝜑 is an isomorphism, the quotient is canonically isomorphic to both Borels. In every case, the kernel J𝜑
measures the complete discrepancy between the geometric and automorphic primitive theories. PBW carries
the quotient to enveloping algebras, Chevalley chains, current envelopes, and Fock characters.
Proof. A quotient identifying 𝑖Δ ( 𝑥 ) with an element of 𝔭+𝑋 defines 𝜑 , and the Lie relations in the quotient make
it a Lie morphism. Conversely, a Lie morphism makes the generators of J𝜑 compatible with every bracket, so
the quotient exists. An isomorphism 𝜑 gives mutually inverse maps through the two retractions. Functoriality of
enveloping algebras, Chevalley chains, currents, and symmetric-superalgebra completion gives the final statement.


   This amalgam is the maximal theory that retains both sources before a duality identification is imposed.
The geometric Hall source and the automorphic Borcherds source are its two canonical retracts. A
microscopic duality is a diagonal quotient rather than an equality inferred from a scalar character.

Chapter 86


Coproducts, pairings, and doubles

A positive Hall algebra becomes a quantum group after additional structure is supplied. Let 𝐻 + be a
complete graded bialgebra, let 𝐻 0 be a Cartan algebra, and let 𝐻 − be the opposite or dual half. Let

                                          ⟨−, −⟩ : 𝐻 + b
                                                       ⊗ 𝐻 − −→ 𝕜

be a nondegenerate Hopf pairing.

Definition 86.1 (Completed Hall double). The completed Drinfeld double is the topological algebra

                                        b ( 𝐻 +) = 𝐻 − b
                                        𝐷                   ⊗ 𝐻+
                                                       ⊗ 𝐻0 b

with internal products in the three factors and cross-relations determined by the Hopf pairing. Its
representation category is monoidal by the coproduct and braided by the universal 𝑅-matrix whenever
the canonical tensor converges.

Theorem 86.2 (Double construction). Let 𝐻 + and 𝐻 − be paired complete graded bialgebras, let 𝐻 0
be a Cartan bialgebra acting by the stated weight grading, and let

                                          ⟨−, −⟩ : 𝐻 + ⊗
                                                       b 𝐻 − −→ 𝕜

be a continuous nondegenerate Hopf pairing. Assume the triangular multiplication map from 𝐻 − b        ⊗
𝐻0 b⊗ 𝐻 + is separated and complete. Then the pairing cross-relations determine a unique completed double
with this triangular decomposition. On every continuous module category on which the canonical tensors
converge, the completed canonical element gives the universal 𝑅-matrix.
Proof. The Hopf pairing defines the cross-relations between the paired halves and makes the three canonical em-
beddings bialgebra morphisms. The Hopf identities identify the two reductions of every triple product, proving
associativity. Separated triangular decomposition gives uniqueness. Finite-degree canonical tensors form a compat-
ible inverse system; convergence on the selected module category yields the quasitriangular element [30, 32].

Theorem 86.3 (Quantum Hall–Borcherds amalgam). Let 𝐻𝑋 and 𝐻Δ be complete Hopf algebras con-
taining the same complete Cartan Hopf algebra 𝐻 0 , with continuous Hopf retractions onto 𝐻 0 . Their
completed amalgamated free product

                                                        ∗ 𝐻 0 𝐻Δ
                                             𝐻𝑋 ⊠Δ = 𝐻𝑋 b

has a unique complete Hopf structure extending the two coproducts, counits, and antipodes. It represents
pairs of continuous Hopf morphisms agreeing on 𝐻 0 and has canonical Hopf retractions to both factors. A
mixed universal 𝑅-matrix is equivalent to a continuous cross-pairing satisfying the two hexagon identities;
the intrinsic 𝑅-matrices of the two retracts remain deﬁned independently.


                                                       215
86. Coproducts, pairings, and doubles                                                                        216


Proof. The algebraic amalgamated free product exists because complete filtered algebras are obtained degreewise
from finite quotients. The two coproducts agree on 𝐻 0 , so the universal property extends them to a coproduct on
the free product. Coassociativity is checked on the two generating Hopf subalgebras. The same argument extends
the counit and antipode. The Hopf retractions are induced by the factor retractions onto 𝐻 0 . A quasitriangular
structure extending both factors requires the additional commutation data between the two generating halves;
writing this data as a cross-pairing turns the quasitriangular and Yang–Baxter equations into the two hexagon
identities.

    The deconcatenation coproduct on a bar complex and the representation-theoretic coproduct on a
quantum group occupy different structures. A geometric comparison between them is a theorem about
a selected model.

Ordered chiral bialgebras

Let 𝐴 be an ordered chiral algebra on a curve 𝐶 . An ordered chiral bialgebra consists of a coproduct

                                            Δ : 𝐴 −→ 𝐴 b
                                                       ⊗𝐴

that is a morphism of ordered chiral algebras, together with counit and the chosen completion. An
antipode upgrades it to a Hopf object. A spectral coproduct may take values in Laurent series in a
difference parameter.

Theorem 87.1 (Representation fusion). A completed coproduct deﬁnes a tensor product of continuous
𝐴-modules by restriction of scalars. A universal 𝑅-matrix satisfying quasitriangularity deﬁnes a braid
operator on that module category.
Proof. For modules 𝑀 , 𝑁 , pull back the 𝐴 ⊗ b 𝐴 action on 𝑀 ⊗ b 𝑁 along Δ. Coassociativity supplies the asso-
ciator. The relation 𝑅Δ ( 𝑎) = Δop ( 𝑎) 𝑅 makes the flip composed with 𝑅 an intertwiner, and the Yang–Baxter
equation gives the braid relation.

The tripled Jordan quiver and ℂ 3

Let 𝑄 have one vertex and loops 𝑥, 𝑦, 𝑧 , with potential

                                             𝑊 = Tr 𝑥 [ 𝑦, 𝑧 ] .

The Jacobi algebra is ℂ[ 𝑥, 𝑦, 𝑧 ]. The critical CoHA uses the vanishing cycles of Tr 𝑊 on the represen-
tation spaces. Its positive currents are generated by the one-vertex dimension sectors.

89.1   Two separated point charges
Two point modules with different first coordinates have no extensions between them. Their Hall corre-
spondence can therefore be computed before the collision terms of the affine Yangian enter. For example,
take 𝑝 = (0, 0, 0) and 𝑞 = (1, 0, 0) in ℂ3 . The module O 𝑝 ⊕ O𝑞 has exactly two submodules of length
one. Ordering these two submodules produces the two terms of the Hall product. The coefficient sheaf
still requires a calculation: the potential is quadratic transverse to this family of commuting triples.
     Work over ℂ, with rational constructible coefficients on analytic quotient stacks. Cohomology means
ordinary equivariant hypercohomology, defined through the Borel construction. There is no torus acting
on the three spatial coordinates in this calculation. The general linear groups record changes of basis of
modules. Set

−𝛿 . Its determinant on the four original normal coordinates is +1. The determinant of the displayed change to

    The Tate factor can also be recorded before forgetting mixed Hodge structures. The affine quadric
𝑢1 𝑣1 + 𝑢2 𝑣2 = 1 is isomorphic to 𝑆 𝐿2 (ℂ). Projection to its first column is an affine-line bundle over
ℂ2 \ {0}. The localization sequence at the origin gives its third cohomology as ℚ(−2). Accordingly,
the untwisted mixed-Hodge version of the lemma reads 𝑠 ∗ P2 = (P1 ⊠ P1 ) (−2). Multiplication in
that coefficient theory must retain this Tate factor, or specify a compensating dimension-dependent
normalization. The rational operations below use the stated forgetful convention.
    The product can be defined on this open correspondence without extending a class across the collision

Theorem 89.3 (Hall multiplication and ordered splitting). On the separated critical locus, Hall induction
is the ﬁnite-cover trace 𝑠∗ . The operations satisfy

                                     𝑚Δ1,1 = 2 id 𝐻𝑆 ,         Δ1,1 𝑚 = id 𝐻𝐷 + 𝜏 ∗ .
Both maps have rank one on the coeﬃcient ﬁbres ℚ2 ⇄ ℚ. The splitting map is injective, and 𝑚 (1) = 2.
For the local system A = 𝑠∗ ℚ𝔇 on 𝔖, the relative pairing

                                   A ⊗ A −→ ℚ𝔖 ,                ( 𝑓 , 𝑔 ) ↦−→ Tr 𝑠 ( 𝑓 𝑔 )

is perfect. Trace and pullback are adjoint for this pairing and ordinary multiplication on ℚ𝔖 .
Proof. The smooth ambient extension stack consists of triples preserving a line. Its map to 𝔛1 × 𝔛1 has relative
dimension 9 − 3 − 4 = 2. The forgetful map is proper because the line lies in the projective variety ℙ1 . The potential
on the extension stack is the sum of the two diagonal potentials. This is the quiver induction correspondence in
[51, §1, equation (1.1)].
      In the ordered diagonal slice, preservation of the first eigenline sets 𝑏21 = 𝑐21 = 0. This is the plane 𝑣1 = 𝑣2 = 0 in
(89.1). Hyperbolic restriction for the cocharacter diag( 𝑡, 1) integrates over this attracting plane. Its complex Thom
class generates 𝐻𝑐4 (ℂ2 , ℚ) and has integral one. The quadratic vanishing cycle has rank one, and its identification
with this Thom class is exactly the orientation fixed above. Consequently the induction coefficient on each ordered
critical sheet is +1. The critical extension stack itself is 𝔇 by Lemma 89.1. Proper pushforward therefore sums the
two sheet values. This proves 𝑚 = 𝑠∗ and identifies the operation with critical Hall induction in this coefficient
convention.
      The fibre product of the cover with itself is the disjoint union of the graphs of 1 and 𝜏 . Finite base change gives
𝑠 ∗ 𝑠∗ = 1 + 𝜏 ∗ . The trace of a pulled-back section is twice that section, giving 𝑠∗ 𝑠 ∗ = 2. On a trivializing open, the

The relative pairing has matrix diag(1, 1) in the two sheet idempotents. This proves perfection and adjointness. All

Proposition 89.4 (Equivariant cohomology and its trace form). Let 𝑢𝑖 = 𝑐1 ( 𝐿 𝑖 ) have degree two. There
is a degree-one class 𝜂 such that
                              ^                          ^
        𝐻𝐷 = ℚ[ 𝑢1 , 𝑢2 ] ⊗ ( 𝜂 ) ,  𝐻𝑆 = ℚ[ 𝑐1 , 𝑐2 ] ⊗ ( 𝜂 ) , 𝑐1 = 𝑢1 + 𝑢2 , 𝑐2 = 𝑢1 𝑢2 .

Here 𝑠 ∗ is the displayed inclusion, 𝜏 ∗ 𝜂 = 𝜂 , and 𝑚 ( 𝑓 ) = 𝑓 + 𝜏 ∗ 𝑓 . In particular,

                       𝑚 ( 𝑢1 ) = 𝑐1 ,     𝑚 ( 𝑢1 𝑢2 ) = 2𝑐2 ,     ker 𝑚 = ( 𝑢1 − 𝑢2 ) 𝐻𝑆 .

As an 𝐻𝑆 -module, 𝐻𝐷 is free with basis 1, 𝑑 , where 𝑑 = 𝑢1 − 𝑢2 . Its cohomological trace pairing has matrix
                                                                !
                                               2        0
                                                                  .
                                               0 2( 𝑐12 − 4𝑐2 )

Thus this pairing is perfect after inverting 𝑐12 − 4𝑐2 . It is not perfect over the displayed polynomial coeﬃcient
ring.
Proof. The coordinates 𝑝 𝑥 − 𝑞 𝑥 , 𝑞 𝑥 , 𝑝𝑦 , 𝑝 𝑧 , 𝑞𝑦 , 𝑞 𝑧 identify 𝐷 with ℂ∗ × ℂ5 . Its cohomology is the exterior
algebra on the winding class 𝜂 of the first coordinate. Multiplication of that coordinate by −1 is homotopic to
the identity, so 𝜏 ∗ 𝜂 = 𝜂 . The Borel construction adds ℚ[ 𝑢1 , 𝑢2 ]. Rational descent through the finite group 𝑆2
takes invariants and has no higher group cohomology. The fundamental theorem of symmetric polynomials gives
the expression for 𝐻𝑆 . Every polynomial splits into its symmetric and antisymmetric parts. The latter is divisible
by 𝑢1 − 𝑢2 , with symmetric quotient. This proves the module basis and kernel formula. Finally, 𝑑 2 = 𝑐12 − 4𝑐2 ,
Tr 𝑠 ( 𝑑 ) = 0, and Tr 𝑠 (1) = 2. The determinant is 4( 𝑐12 − 4𝑐2 ), which is a nonunit before localization.

    The last discriminant concerns equivariant Chern classes. It differs from the spatial difference 𝑝 𝑥 − 𝑞 𝑥 ,
already inverted in the definition of 𝐷 . A perfect pairing on a finite-cover coefficient sheaf can therefore
induce a nonperfect pairing on its equivariant cohomology. Integration over the nonproper base and its
𝐵 𝔾𝑚 stabilizers requires further support and localization data before it yields a scalar Hall pairing.
    In the point-brane interpretation, the Koszul contraction removes open-string cross modes between
separated branes. The four transverse matrix coordinates form two quadratic pairs. The two terms of
𝑚 (1) count the choices of a constituent line in their direct sum. This interpretation uses the algebraic
point-module sector and does not identify it with renormalized holomorphic Chern–Simons observ-
ables.
    If a global Hall coproduct restricts to Δ1,1 , every length-two primitive restricts to zero on 𝔖, by (89.3).
The collision locus requires new data: at 𝑝 = 𝑞 , the cross Koszul differential is zero and its cohomology
dimensions are 1, 3, 3, 1. The spectral projectors and the transverse quadratic reduction then cease to apply.
Extending multiplication and splitting across collisions, comparing them with the chosen equivariant
shuffle coproduct, and constructing a continuous scalar Hopf pairing are separate requirements for a
geometric Hall double. The compact Calabi–Yau comparison also requires orientation descent and a
map from the chosen field-theoretic observables.

89.2     The equivariant positive algebra
Theorem 89.5 (Affine-Yangian positive half). After the standard equivariant localization and completion,
the critical CoHA of the tripled Jordan quiver is identiﬁed with the positive half of the aﬃne Yangian of
𝔤𝔩 1 . The independently deﬁned aﬃne Yangian carries its standard Cartan and negative current halves;
the positive-half comparison embeds the CoHA into that triangular presentation.



226
227                                                              89.2. The equivariant positive algebra


Proof. The CoHA product becomes the shuffle product under localization. Dimensional reduction identifies the
tripled-quiver critical theory with the equivariant surface CoHA, and Davison’s affine-BPS theorem identifies its
completed positive algebra with the strictly positive affine Yangian. The shuffle kernel satisfies the affine-Yangian
current relations with equivariant parameters 𝜖1 , 𝜖2 , 𝜖3 and Calabi–Yau relation 𝜖1 + 𝜖2 + 𝜖3 = 0. PBW and current
comparison identify the positive algebra. The independently presented full affine Yangian is the triangular algebra
generated by its negative currents, Cartan currents, and this positive half [23, 25, 70, 71].

    The standard affine Yangian admits distinguished vacuum representations and their vertex-algebra
realizations. A geometric comparison with its full double requires the corresponding coproduct and
scalar Hopf pairing. Theorem 89.3 constructs ordered splitting on the separated rank-two locus. Proposi-
tion 89.4 specifies the localization needed even for the cohomological trace form in that locus. Extension
across collisions remains part of the full geometric comparison.

Chapter 90


Surface CoHAs and ADE Yangians

Let 𝑆Γ → ℂ2 /Γ be the minimal resolution for a finite subgroup Γ ⊂ SL2 (ℂ). The exceptional curves
have ADE intersection matrix. Moduli of sheaves supported on these curves carry convolution corre-
spondences.

Theorem 90.1 (Curve-supported surface CoHA). The completed positive CoHA of one-dimensional
sheaves supported on the exceptional curves is identiﬁed with a nonstandard positive half of the aﬃne
Yangian of the corresponding ADE Lie algebra.
Proof. The theorem is the curve-supported surface construction of Diaconescu–Porta–Sala–Schiffmann–
Vasserot [27]. The simple curve sheaves give the simple generators, Hecke correspondences give the current
operators, and the completed PBW comparison gives the affine Yangian presentation.

   This theorem is a local K3 model whenever an ADE configuration occurs on a K3 surface. The global
K3 surface adds compact curve classes and monodromy beyond the local root lattice.




                                                   229
Chapter 91


Coulomb branches and shifted Yangians

For a three-dimensional N = 4 gauge theory, the BFN Coulomb branch is defined by equivariant
Borel–Moore homology of an affine-Grassmannian correspondence. Its convolution algebra quantizes
the Coulomb branch [12]. In quiver gauge theories, shifted Yangians and their truncations provide
presentations of these quantizations. Boundary conditions select the shift and truncation data.
    The BFN construction supplies a second geometric source of Yangians. Its grading and coproduct
arise from loop rotation and convolution, while the surface CoHA source arises from sheaf extensions.
A comparison between the two is a model-specific equivalence.

The orthogonal LLV algebra

For a compact hyperkähler manifold, every Lefschetz class gives an 𝔰𝔩 2 triple on cohomology. The Lie
algebra generated by all such triples is the Looijenga–Lunts–Verbitsky algebra.

Theorem 94.1 (Orthogonal LLV symmetries). For a K3 surface 𝑆 , the LLV algebra acting on 𝐻 ∗ ( 𝑆 ) is
                                                                 
                              𝔤LLV ( 𝑆 )  𝔰𝔬 𝐻
                                              e ( 𝑆 ) , (−, −)Muk  𝔰𝔬(4, 20) .

For an irreducible hyperkähler manifold 𝑀 of 𝐾 3 [ 𝑛 ] type with 𝑛 ≥ 2, one has 𝑏2 ( 𝑀 ) = 23 and

gives signature (4, 21) [57, 72].

   The LLV algebra is a classical cohomological symmetry. A Yangian deformation of the full orthogonal
action consists of two further structures: a Drinfeld current presentation and a compatible spectral
coproduct.




                                                       239
Chapter 95


Local ADE Yangians

An ADE configuration of (−2)-curves on 𝑆 gives a root sublattice of 𝐻 2 ( 𝑆, ℤ). Sheaves supported
on the configuration produce the surface CoHA of the preceding part and hence a completed positive
affine ADE Yangian. Nakajima quiver varieties supply compatible Yangian actions on their equivariant
cohomology.
    Thus K3 geometry contains local nonabelian Yangian sectors indexed by ADE enhancements of the
Picard lattice. Picard monodromy transports the ADE root sublattices over the corresponding Noether–
Lefschetz loci. A family of sheaf moduli and Hecke correspondences compatible with that monodromy
transports the Yangian actions themselves.

A global K3 quantum algebra is a system that contains these three actions with explicit comparison

The K3 correspondence category

Let 𝑆 be a projective K3 surface. Let M 𝑆 contain the smooth projective moduli spaces 𝑀𝜎 ( 𝑣 ) of stable
objects for primitive Mukai vectors and generic stability conditions, the Hilbert schemes 𝑆 [ 𝑛 ] , and
the equivariant curve-supported moduli at every ADE enhancement. Close this collection under finite
products.

Definition 97.1 (K3 correspondence category). The category Corr 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) has the spaces of M 𝑆 as objects
and
                                Hom( 𝑀 , 𝑁 ) = 𝐻∗BM ( 𝑀 × 𝑁 )
for proper objects, with the corresponding localized equivariant Borel–Moore theory on ADE charts.
Composition is convolution:                                    
                                       𝛽 ◦ 𝛼 = ( 𝑝13 )∗ 𝑝12∗ 𝛼 ∩ 𝑝23
                                                                  ∗

The tensor product is Cartesian product of moduli spaces and external product of correspondences.

Theorem 97.2 (Associativity and monoidality). Transverse convolution makes Corr 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) a graded
monoidal category. The cohomology functor

                                            𝜔𝑆 ( 𝑀 ) = 𝐻 ∗ ( 𝑀 )

and its localized equivariant form on ADE charts is strong monoidal and carries convolution to composition
of linear operators.
Proof. The two parenthesizations of three correspondences are the two push–pull descriptions on 𝑀1 × 𝑀2 ×
𝑀3 × 𝑀4 . Proper base change and the projection formula identify them. Cartesian products and external products
satisfy the interchange law. The standard action of a kernel on cohomology is compatible with both operations.

   Nakajima incidence varieties are morphisms in this category. Cup product and Lefschetz adjoints are
diagonal kernels. ADE Hecke correspondences are equivariant morphisms. Thus the three K3 symmetry
families occupy one correspondence category before passage to any enveloping algebra.

Theorem 98.2 (Exact K3 symmetry envelope). The algebra A 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) is separated and complete. It contains
the Nakajima Heisenberg, every LLV Lefschetz triple, monodromy transport, and every local ADE Yangian
action. The cross-relations are the intersection identities of their kernels. This description is faithful by
construction.
Proof. Each named operator is induced by a correspondence in Corr 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ). Convolution gives their products. The
image quotient removes precisely the correspondences acting trivially on all selected cohomologies, and closure
gives completeness.

   The three symmetries retain their intrinsic forms inside one algebra. Their interaction is calculated
by intersections of the corresponding cycles. On a Noether–Lefschetz locus, an LLV operator and an
ADE Hecke operator meet in the same correspondence space; the resulting convolution is the geometric
cross-relation.

            be the rigid Karoubi envelope of the dualizable correspondence objects, modulo the tensor

                                           𝜔 𝑆 : C 𝑆 −→ sVectfd

Definition 99.1 (K3 correspondence quantum group). The coordinate bialgebra is the coend
                                                 ∫ 𝑀 ∈C 𝑆
                                  O (𝔾 𝐾 3 ) =              𝜔𝑆 ( 𝑀 )∗ ⊗ 𝜔𝑆 ( 𝑀 ) .

Its continuous dual is denoted 𝑈 (𝔾 𝐾 3 ).

Theorem 99.2 (K3 Tannaka theorem). On every ﬁnite Mukai-norm truncation, C 𝑆 is equivalent to the
rigid exact tensor subcategory of ﬁnite comodules over O (𝔾 𝐾 3 ) generated by the universal coactions on the
ﬁbres 𝜔 𝑆 ( 𝑀 ). Every geometric K3 correspondence induces a comodule morphism. The continuous dual
acts naturally on every cohomology ﬁbre and represents tensor-compatible natural endomorphisms of the
ﬁbre functor. A braided transport on C 𝑆 produces a coquasitriangular structure on the coend.
Proof. Exact rigid coend reconstruction applies to the faithful exact strong monoidal functor 𝜔 𝑆 . The coend carries
the universal coaction on each fibre. The exact tensor envelope used to define C 𝑆 is precisely the subcategory
generated from these comodules by the operations preserved by 𝜔 𝑆 , which gives the stated equivalence. The
quotient map from geometric correspondences makes each correspondence a comodule morphism. Dualizing the
universal coaction represents tensor-compatible natural endomorphisms. A braiding supplies the bilinear form
whose hexagon identities are the coquasitriangular axioms.

   The action algebra A 𝐾 3 ( 𝑆 ) and the Tannaka algebra 𝑈 (𝔾 𝐾 3 ) are complementary. The first is gen-
erated by geometric kernels. The second consists of tensor symmetries compatible with every kernel.
Their commutants record two complementary aspects of the K3 correspondence geometry: geometric
operators and tensor-compatible natural symmetries.

Theorem 100.4 (Integral source–target completion). The integral protected avatar retains the geometric
extension theory, the universal Igusa root theory, and their common polarized charge lattice in one functorial
system. The retraction 𝑟 𝑋 recovers the geometric BPS Borel, while 𝑟Δ recovers the Borcherds Borel and its

quotient arises in this way.

The noncommutative Calabi–Yau field theory

Every finite-height Ginzburg algebra ΓΔ,𝐻 has a cyclic three-Calabi–Yau pairing. Its open string field is a

                                                      259
Chapter 104


Physical meaning

The protected avatar is the complete universal algebraic model selected by the Igusa root datum. The
Ginzburg category supplies its noncommutative three-Calabi–Yau sector. The quiver Hall category
supplies its ordinary extension geometry. The Borcherds root superspace supplies its protected one-
particle states. The colored Ran object supplies its local and wrapped current algebra on the elliptic
curve. Hong’s quantum Borcherds algebra supplies its line-operator symmetry. The root Dirac family
supplies its first-order determinant, and the Fock construction supplies the scalar square.
   A compact string construction with specified simple-root states and extension amplitudes maps to
the protected avatar through the presentation universal property. The comparison is determined on
primitives; PBW propagates it to multiparticle states, the Hall product, the current algebra, the quantum
double, and the determinant.




                                                  261
Chapter 105


Universal property of the protected avatar

A presentation-preserving protected theory consists of a three-Calabi–Yau category, an oriented Hall
boundary, a colored elliptic factorization algebra, a quasitriangular quantum symmetry, an oriented
determinant, and comparison functors from the Borcherds generators-and-relations construction to
these five objects. The chosen simple-root states satisfy the Gritsenko–Nikulin Cartan, parity, and Serre
relations.

Theorem 105.1 (Presentation universal property). Every presentation-preserving protected theory carries
a unique primitive Lie-superalgebra morphism

                                           𝔫Δ5 ,+ −→ Prim(𝔗)

extending the chosen simple-root states. The Hall, Ginzburg, current, quantum-group, and determinant
functors extend this primitive morphism to the ﬁve selected comparison maps. A primitive isomorphism
together with equivalences of the selected functorial realizations gives an equivalence of protected data.
Proof. The Borcherds generators and relations give the unique primitive morphism. Each remaining construction
is functorial in a presentation map: quiver Hall convolution in the quiver map, Calabi–Yau completion in the
dg-algebra map, chiral Chevalley theory in the Lie map, Hong quantization in the Cartan superdatum, and the
diagonal Fredholm operator in the root-graded map. PBW promotes the primitive isomorphism to the completed
enveloping and Fock objects.

Proof. A full inclusion of root data adds vertices and arrows to the Borcherds quiver. Keller completion and

Borcherds superalgebra is compatible with root truncation. Proper height makes each coefficient eventually con-

bra.

From positive half to full quantum group

Suppose the compact or local positive algebra 𝐻 + is paired continuously with a negative half 𝐻 − , carries
a completed coproduct, and admits Cartan data and a separated triangular completion. The completed
double
                                             U=𝐷   b ( 𝐻 +)

has triangular decomposition 𝐻 − b   ⊗ 𝐻0 ⊗b 𝐻 + . Its universal 𝑅-matrix acts on continuous tensor prod-
ucts of modules. The classical limit is a Lie bialgebra whose cobracket is the first-order term of the
coproduct asymmetry.
   For ℂ3 , 𝐻 + is the positive affine Yangian. For local ADE surfaces, it is the completed positive affine
ADE Yangian. For the Igusa algebra, the paired Borel construction of the preceding part supplies a height-
complete formal quantum group; a compact Hall realization maps to it through primitive, coproduct,
and pairing comparisons.

Tannaka reconstruction

Let L be an essentially small rigid 𝕜-linear abelian monoidal category with finite-dimensional morphism
spaces, finite-length objects, and exact tensor product. Let

                                             𝜔 : L → Vectfd

be a faithful exact strong monoidal functor. Assume the coend
                                           ∫ 𝑀∈L
                                     𝐻=             𝜔 ( 𝑀 )∗ ⊗ 𝜔 ( 𝑀 )

exists and that the canonical comparison functor to finite-dimensional 𝐻 -comodules is comonadic. Ten-
sor product makes 𝐻 a bialgebra, and rigidity supplies its antipode. A braiding gives a coquasitriangular
form; a meromorphic braiding gives its spectral version.

Theorem 108.1 (Rigid Tannaka reconstruction). Under the preceding ﬁnite and comonadic hypotheses,
the comparison functor is a monoidal equivalence

                                           L ≃ Comodfd ( 𝐻 ) .

Braiding transports to the universal coquasitriangular form on 𝐻 .
Proof. Matrix coefficients define the universal 𝐻 -coaction on every 𝜔 ( 𝑀 ). Tensor product and duality give
the bialgebra and antipode structures. The Barr–Beck comonadic theorem identifies L with coalgebras for the
induced comonad, and the coend identifies that comonad with 𝐻 ⊗ −. The hexagon identities transport to the
coquasitriangular identities.

   This reconstruction explains why a line-operator category, a fiber functor, and exchange together
produce a quantum group. The ordered boundary algebra alone provides fusion; the plane of exchange
supplies braiding.

                                                  273
Chapter 110


The centre and the double

The Hochschild centre 𝑍 ord ( 𝐴) is the universal E2 algebra acting centrally on an ordered algebra. The
Drinfeld centre 𝑍 (Mod 𝐴 ) is the braided category of modules equipped with half-braidings. Under
dualizability hypotheses these are related by the passage from algebraic Hochschild cochains to categorical
endomorphisms of the identity. Derived loop-space descriptions of centres provide the geometric form
of this relation [6].
    The centre and the double answer complementary questions. The centre classifies central bulk actions.
The double reconstructs a full quantum group from paired positive and negative halves. A field theory
can supply a comparison by realizing both as line and bulk operator constructions.
