353                                                       134.4. Regular matrices and a fivefold collision


The algebra is free over ℂ[ 𝑒 ] with basis 1, 𝑎, 𝑎2 , 𝑏, 𝑎𝑏, 𝑏 2 . Here is a direct division proof. Adjoin
                                    𝑔3 = 𝑎 𝑔2 − 𝑏 𝑔1 = 𝑎𝑏 2 − 𝑎𝑒 4 − 𝑏 2 𝑒 + 𝑏𝑒 3 ,
                                    𝑔4 = 𝑎 𝑔3 − 𝑏 𝑔2 = 𝑏 3 − 𝑎2 𝑒 4 + 𝑎𝑏𝑒 3 − 𝑏 2 𝑒 2 + 𝑏𝑒 4 .
Order monomials in 𝑎, 𝑏 by (deg 𝑎 +2 deg𝑏 , − deg𝑏 ), with 𝑒 a coefficient. Their leading monomials are
𝑎3 , 𝑎2 𝑏, 𝑎𝑏 2 , 𝑏 3 , all with coefficient one. The adjacent leading-term cancellations are
             𝑏 𝑔1 − 𝑎 𝑔2 = − 𝑔3 ,         𝑏 𝑔2 − 𝑎 𝑔3 = − 𝑔4 ,        𝑏 𝑔3 − 𝑎 𝑔4 = 𝑒 4 𝑔1 − 𝑒 3 𝑔2 + 𝑒 2 𝑔3 − 𝑒 𝑔4 .
Every term on the right has smaller leading monomial than the corresponding cancellation. Nonadjacent cancel-
lations are telescoping sums of these adjacent ones. Division therefore gives a unique remainder in the six listed
monomials. No division by 𝑒 occurs. Adjoining 𝑒 5 = 𝑠 makes the algebra free of rank thirty over ℂ[ 𝑠 ]. At 𝑠 = 0 its
only geometric point has 𝑒 = 𝑎 = 𝑏 = 0.
    For 𝑒 ≠ 0, put 𝜁 = exp(2 𝜋 𝑖 /5). The four remaining roots are 𝑒𝜁 𝑖 , 1 ≤ 𝑖 ≤ 4. Choosing two gives
                                     𝑎 = −𝑒 ( 𝜁 𝑖 + 𝜁 𝑗 ) ,      𝑏 = 𝑒 2 𝜁 𝑖 +𝑗 ,     𝑖 < 𝑗.
These are six distinct smooth branches parameterized by 𝑒 . They meet only at zero. The algebra is generically
reduced, and its freeness over ℂ[ 𝑠 ] excludes any nilpotent supported at zero. Thus these are its reduced analytic
branches as well. Following a loop in 𝑠 sends 𝑒 to 𝜁 𝑒 on each branch, which proves the asserted six five-cycles.

    For this family the input and output 𝑢 and 𝑒 trivializations agree: 𝑒2 ⊠ 𝑒2 ⊠ 𝑒1 = 𝑢2 ⊠ 𝑢2 ⊠ 𝑢1 and
𝑒5 = 𝑢5 . This equality holds over the central point as well, by the cyclic construction above. The following
trace uses these identical trivializations.
Proposition 134.4 (Coefficient specialization at the collision). Pulling 𝑀𝐿 = 𝑀221 = 𝑀𝑅 back to this
disk gives one morphism
                                     𝑅𝜋∗ ℚ𝑌 [10] −→ ℚ𝐷 [10] .
On 𝐷 \ {0} it is the thirty-sheet trace. At zero it is multiplication by thirty. Let 𝑗 : 𝐷 \ {0} ↩→ 𝐷 and
𝑖 : {0} ↩→ 𝐷 be the inclusions. If 𝐿 is the rank-twenty-nine kernel of the sheet trace, then
                  𝑅𝜋∗ ℚ𝑌 = ℚ𝐷 ⊕ 𝑗! 𝐿,                  dim 𝐻 0 ( 𝑖 ∗ 𝑅𝑗∗ 𝐿 ) = dim 𝐻 1 ( 𝑖 ∗ 𝑅𝑗∗ 𝐿 ) = 5.
In particular 𝑗! 𝐿 and 𝑅𝑗∗ 𝐿 are different complexes.
Proof. The restrictions to the submodule and successive quotients are cyclic modules with the three monic charac-
teristic factors. Lemma 134.2 identifies their coefficient product with ℚ𝑌 [10] and the target with ℚ𝐷 [10]. Proper
base change gives the displayed actual coefficient morphism. Finite fibres have no higher rational cohomology, so
𝑅𝜋∗ ℚ𝑌 is a sheaf in degree zero. Its central stalk is ℚ, and its nearby stalk is ℚ30 . Specialization is the diagonal
map: a constant near the common branch point restricts to the same constant on all thirty nearby points.
    The target specialization is the identity. Compatibility of the constructed morphism with specialization and
the generic sheet trace forces its central value to be thirty. This determines the whole morphism: the target constant
sheaf injects into its restriction and direct image from the punctured disk, so a morphism vanishing there is zero.
    The constant-section map ℚ𝐷 → 𝜋∗ ℚ𝑌 , followed by the trace divided by thirty, is the identity. Its comple-
mentary kernel has zero central stalk and restricts to 𝐿 . The adjunction map 𝑗! 𝐿 to that kernel is an isomorphism
on every stalk.
    Let 𝑊 be a fibre of 𝐿 and 𝑇 its positive-circle monodromy. The punctured disk retracts onto a circle. Its
                            𝑇 −1
cellular cochains are 𝑊 −−−→ 𝑊 , in degrees zero and one. A fixed vector in the thirty-sheet space is constant
on each of the six five-cycles. The trace-zero condition imposes one independent relation on those six constants.
Thus the kernel of 𝑇 − 1 on 𝑊 has dimension five. Its cokernel has the same dimension by rank-nullity. This
proves both displayed nearby-direct-image groups. The stalk of 𝑗! 𝐿 at zero is zero in every degree, proving the last
assertion.


                                                                                                                        353
134. A flag with two planes and a line                                                                     354


   The five-cycle action is monodromy in the base parameter 𝑠 . Potential monodromy is a different action.
On the cyclic normal quadratic it is the antipodal map of 𝑆 39 and has degree +1. The support construction
intertwines the latter action and also commutes with continuation in 𝑠 . The trace calculation retains
both actions and their specialization maps.

134.5    Scalar coefficients and the remaining geometric comparison
At a scalar five-dimensional triple every flag is invariant. The incoming complex is the family P2 ( 𝐻 ) ⊗
P2 ( 𝐾 / 𝐻 ) ⊗ P1 (ℂ5 / 𝐾 ) on Fl(2, 4; 5), with all frame actions retained. At an ordinary atlas point, each
rank-two scalar coefficient has degrees −4, −2, 1. The rank-one coefficient contributes degree −2. Thus
its nine ordered products have

                                   degree −10 −8 −6 −5 −3 0
                                    rank    1 2   1 2 2 1.

The top product is the tensor of two odd degree-one classes and the degree-minus-two line class. In-
terchanging the two plane coefficients gives sign −1. Associating them in two orders introduces no
interchange and gives the equality proved above.
    This is a calculation of incoming coefficient stalks. It does not trivialize the family on the flag variety
or determine its proper direct image. The scalar target P5 , the image of 𝑀221 there, and its extension
data require further calculations. The cyclic Jordan collision has a nonzero nilpotent matrix and belongs
to a different stratum.
    For point modules, eigenvalues specify positions in ℂ3 and nilpotent parts specify extensions at
coincident positions. The two plane constituents retain cubic critical fluctuations, including their odd
scalar coefficient. The flag construction couples those coefficients to the five-dimensional module in
two orders with the same result. This proves the stated rational critical Hall associativity comparison.
Additional block sizes, higher coherence, a coproduct, Tate refinements and a compact Calabi–Yau
field-theory identification remain separate constructions.

Chapter 137


Divisor, character, and the meaning of the
square root

The diagonal Humbert divisor is

                                     𝐻1 = { 𝑍 ∈ ℍ2 : 𝑧 = 0}/Sp4 (ℤ) .

Theorem 137.1 (Diagonal divisor). The divisor of 𝐷5 is the Sp4 (ℤ)-orbit of 𝐻1 , with multiplicity one. The
form transforms by the Maass character 𝜈5 of order two, and 𝜒10 = 𝐷5 2 is scalar-valued.
Proof. This is the defining diagonal-divisor property of the Igusa square root and the first row of the Cléry–
Gritsenko classification [17, 46, 47].

    Real roots of the denominator algebra define Weyl reflections. Imaginary roots contribute root
spaces and product exponents. This division of labor is intrinsic to a Borcherds algebra: the Weyl group
is generated by real simple roots, while the imaginary simple roots enter the correction term of the
denominator identity.

137.1   Automorphic section, orientation line, and Pfaffian operator
An automorphic square root is a section of a line bundle with character. An orientation is a square root
of a determinant line. A Pfaffian operator is a skew family whose regularized Pfaffian is a section of that
orientation line. These form a sequence of successive refinements:

                        automorphic section −→ determinant-line identification
                −→ oriented square-root line −→ Fredholm family and Pfaffian section.

Proposition 137.2 (Logical separation of the four levels). The product theorem constructs the ﬁrst object
in the sequence. A determinant-line comparison constructs the second. A coherent square root of that line
constructs the third. A ﬁrst-order operator requires a symbol, domain, ellipticity or Fredholm condition,
and regularization, and thereby produces the fourth reﬁnement.
Proof. Each stage adds a specified geometric structure. A determinant realization adds an isomorphism from a
geometric determinant line to the automorphic line. An oriented realization adds a coherent square-root line and
its parity refinement. An operator realization adds a principal symbol, a Fredholm domain, a spectrum, and a
regularization whose Pfaffian is the resulting section.

   The title names the monic automorphic section 𝐷5 , characterized by the exact identity 𝐷5 2 = 𝜒10 .
Part IV formulates an analytic Pfaffian realization as a further geometric refinement.

The Gritsenko–Nikulin Lie superalgebra

The Borcherds product determines a generalized Kac–Moody Lie superalgebra 𝔤Δ5 with real simple
roots 𝛿1 , 𝛿2 , 𝛿3 and an imaginary correction system determined by 𝜙0,1 .

Theorem 139.1 (Root supermultiplicities and denominator). On the active positive support,

                                       smult 𝔤Δ5 𝛼 ( 𝑛,ℓ ,𝑚 ) = 𝑓 ( 𝑛𝑚, ℓ ) .

In the Lorentzian coordinate convention of Gritsenko–Nikulin,

                                   den(𝔤Δ5 ) = 𝐷5 (2 𝑍 ) = 2−6 Δ5𝜃 (2 𝑍 ) .

Proof. The real roots and Weyl chamber are the type-II case of the Gritsenko–Nikulin construction. The imaginary
simple-root correction is chosen so that its Weyl–Kac–Borcherds product has exponent 𝑓 ( 𝑛𝑚, ℓ ). The resulting
product is the doubled-variable monic Igusa form [40–42].

   The theorem gives a Lie superalgebra with a bracket, Cartan, Weyl group, root parity, and denomina-
tor. The scalar product alone records the signed dimensions of the root spaces; the Gritsenko–Nikulin
construction supplies the algebraic operations.

Point-local and wrapped sectors

Let 𝑝 𝐸 : 𝑆 × 𝐸 → 𝐸 be projection.
Lemma 144.1 (Positive elliptic degree is wrapped). Let 𝐶 ⊂ 𝑆 × 𝐸 be a proper one-dimensional subscheme
whose pushforward ( 𝑝 𝐸 )∗ [𝐶 ] has positive degree. Then 𝑝 𝐸 ( 𝐶 ) = 𝐸 .
Proof. Properness makes 𝑝 𝐸 ( 𝐶 ) closed. Positive pushforward degree implies that at least one irreducible compo-
nent of 𝐶 dominates a one-dimensional closed subset of 𝐸 . Every one-dimensional closed subset of the irreducible
curve 𝐸 is 𝐸 itself, so the image of that component, and hence the image of 𝐶 , is all of 𝐸 .

   Projection-finite objects define point-supported sectors on Ran( 𝐸 ). Positive elliptic degree defines
wrapped sectors supported over the entire elliptic curve.
Definition 144.2 (The local–wrapped factorization operad). Let Ch 𝐸 denote the chosen chi-
ral/factorization operad on 𝐸 and let Ass denote the ordered associative operad. Define the colored
operad Hyb 𝐸 with colors
                                       l,    w𝑚 ( 𝑚 ≥ 1)
by the following operation objects.
   (i) Operations with local output are
                                                 Hyb 𝐸 ( l ⊗ 𝑘 ; l) = Ch 𝐸 ( 𝑘 ) .

  (ii) For 𝑟 ≥ 1 and 𝑚 = 𝑚1 + · · · + 𝑚𝑟 , operations with wrapped output are
                                Hyb 𝐸 ( l ⊗ 𝑘 , w𝑚1 , . . . , w𝑚𝑟 ; w𝑚 ) = Ch 𝐸 ( 𝑘 ) ⊗ Ass ( 𝑟 ) .

       All other operation objects are initial.
Operadic composition uses chiral insertion and disjoint union in the first factor and ordered substitution
in the second. The equality
                                ( 𝛾1 ◦ 𝛾2 ) ⊗ ( 𝑎1 ◦ 𝑎2 ) = ( 𝛾1 ⊗ 𝑎1 ) ◦ ( 𝛾2 ⊗ 𝑎2 )
is the mixed interchange law. The arities ( 𝑘, 𝑟 ) = ( 𝑘, 0) , (0, 2) , ( 𝑘, 1) recover, respectively, local factor-
ization, wrapped multiplication, and the action of local insertions on a wrapped sector.
Definition 144.3 (Hybrid local–wrapped algebra). A hybrid local–wrapped algebra over 𝐸 is an al-
gebra over Hyb 𝐸 . It consists of a factorization algebra 𝔽 loc , a height- or charge-complete family 𝑊 =
Q
  𝑚 >0 𝑊𝑚 , ordered products

                               𝜇𝑚1 ,...,𝑚𝑟 : 𝑊𝑚1 ⊗ · · · ⊗ 𝑊𝑚𝑟 −→ 𝑊𝑚1 +···+𝑚𝑟 ,
and factorization-compatible actions of the local operation objects Ch 𝐸 ( 𝑘 ) on every wrapped product.
The operad axioms state associativity, local factorization, and compatibility of the two operations.

The formal Igusa quantum group

                                                                                                ∨,gr
Let 𝔟+ be the positive Borcherds Borel half. Form the restricted graded dual 𝔟− := 𝔟+ with bracket
and cobracket transported by the invariant pairing, identify the two Cartan copies along their common
radical quotient, and complete by a proper root height. The completed Drinfeld double of this paired
Borel datum is denoted 𝔤Δ5 pair . Its positive and negative halves, diagonal Cartan, invariant form, and
canonical tensor form a height-admissible Manin triple and hence a quasitriangular Lie superbialgebra.

Definition 146.1 (Height-admissible Lie superbialgebra). A root-graded Lie superbialgebra is height-
admissible when every root space is finite-dimensional, every bounded-height region contains finitely
many roots, and the bracket, cobracket, invariant tensor, and all iterated operations are coefficientwise
finite in the height completion.

Proposition 146.2 (Height admissibility). The paired Gritsenko–Nikulin Borcherds Lie superbialgebra
𝔤Δ5 pair is height-admissible.
Proof. The Borcherds root spaces are finite-dimensional and the chosen height is proper. The bracket and co-
bracket preserve total root degree. In a bounded total height, finitely many decompositions of a root occur. The
canonical tensor is therefore a coefficientwise finite sum in every height quotient.

Theorem 146.3 (Quantized Borcherds superalgebra). Choose the symmetrizable Borcherds–Cartan
superdatum and multiplicity index set that present 𝔤Δ5 . Hong’s construction gives a quantized enveloping
Hopf superalgebra
                                               𝑈𝑞 (𝔤Δ5 )
over ℚ( 𝑞 ), generated by its Cartan elements and the real and imaginary simple-root generators, with
triangular decomposition

                               𝑈𝑞 (𝔤Δ5 )  𝑈𝑞− (𝔤Δ5 ) ⊗ 𝑈𝑞0 (𝔤Δ5 ) ⊗ 𝑈𝑞+ (𝔤Δ5 ) .

Its PBW root spaces have the same graded ranks as the classical Borcherds superalgebra. The canonical
pairing of the two Borel halves yields a universal 𝑅-matrix in the root-height completion of

                                            𝑈𝑞+ (𝔤Δ5 ) ⊗
                                                       b 𝑈𝑞− (𝔤Δ5 ) .

Consequently the quantization preserves the root-multiplicity data that enter 𝐷5 (2 𝑍 ).
Proof. The Gritsenko–Nikulin algebra is a symmetrizable Borcherds–Kac–Moody Lie superalgebra. Apply
the generators-and-relations construction of the quantized Borcherds superalgebra. The nondegenerate bilinear
form, triangular decomposition, centre, and universal 𝑅-matrix are established by Hong [45]. The PBW theorem
identifies the associated graded root spaces with their classical counterparts. Since the denominator exponents are
the classical signed root multiplicities, they are unchanged by the flat root-graded deformation.

Theorem 147.1 (Classical local and exact zero-mode realization). For every 𝐻 :
   (i) 𝑆 𝐻 satisﬁes the classical master equation and deﬁnes a classical holomorphic–topological factoriza-
       tion algebra;
  (ii) harmonic reduction along a compact interval gives the ﬁnite-dimensional cotangent BV theory of
       𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) , whose positive-root grading makes its modular supercharacter zero and hence gives the
       quantum master equation;
 (iii) after choosing the complementary Lagrangian boundary polarizations, the boundary algebra is
       𝑈 (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) ), the interval amplitude is 1 ⊗𝑈𝕃 (𝔫 ( 𝐻 ) ) 1 ≃ 𝐶∗ (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) ), and the holomorphic

       boundary current envelope is 𝑉𝐻 .
                                                             bℎ (𝔫Δ ,+ ), interval complex 𝐶 ∧ (𝔫Δ ,+ ),
The transition maps yield a pro-object with boundary algebra 𝑈     5                        ∗,ℎ   5

and current envelope 𝑉
                     bΔ .
                          5


Proof. The classical master equation is the Jacobi identity and invariance of the cotangent pairing. The classical
observables satisfy factorization descent. Harmonic reduction is finite-dimensional. An even adjoint operator raises
positive-root height and is nilpotent on the finite quotient; an odd adjoint operator is odd. Both have supertrace
zero, so the modular term in the finite BV Laplacian vanishes. The chosen polarization quantizes the boundary
Lie–Poisson bracket to the enveloping algebra; the two augmentation states give the two-sided bar, identified
with Chevalley chains by PBW. Holomorphic descendants give the current algebra. All structure maps preserve
height.

   The theorem gives the precise field-theoretic realization used in this volume: a classical local theory
together with an exact quantum protected zero-mode sector.

Remark 148.3. The polarization and orientation in this theorem belong to the root Hilbert space. A
compact moduli-space orientation is a separate line bundle and is compared with this one by a specified
intertwiner.
    This theorem constructs the square-root operator on the universal Borcherds root Hilbert superspace.
A geometric realization is a determinant-line intertwiner from a protected geometric Hilbert complex
to this universal operator family.

Chapter 149


What the automorphic character determines

Theorem 149.1 (Denominator data and reconstruction data). The Borcherds product determines:
   (i) the signed root multiplicity sdim 𝔤Δ5 𝛼 in every active root degree;
  (ii) the Weyl vector and denominator character;
 (iii) the divisor and automorphic multiplier of the section.
A Lie bracket is supplied by the Borcherds–Kac–Moody presentation, parity dimensions by a parity-reﬁned
root datum, Hall multiplication by extension correspondences, and an oriented square root by a square-root
line with monodromy. These reconstruction data reﬁne the scalar product independently.
Proof. The first three items are read from the exponent, prefactor, and automorphy law. Independence follows
from explicit pairs: two brackets on the same graded vector space have the same PBW character; an even–odd
pair has signed dimension zero; distinct Hall correspondences may have the same Euler product; and twisting a
square-root line by a sign local system preserves its square. Hence each refined object is reconstructed from the
additional datum named in the statement.

Recognition Theorem 149.2 (Complete primitive recognition). Let 𝔭 be a positive graded Lie superal-
gebra and let Ψ : 𝔫Δ5 ,+ ↠ 𝔭 be a graded Lie map. The map is an isomorphism if either:
   (i) the parity-reﬁned Hilbert series of 𝑈 (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ) and 𝑈 (𝔭) agree; or
  (ii) each primitive degree has prescribed pure parity and the signed PBW characters agree.
The induced height completions, Chevalley complexes, and current envelopes are then isomorphic.
Proof. Choose the first height containing a homogeneous kernel vector. All decomposable PBW terms in that
height are determined by lower heights and agree. The primitive parity dimension therefore drops, contradicting
the first hypothesis. In the second case the logarithm of the PBW product recovers signed primitive dimensions
inductively, and prescribed parity recovers ordinary dimensions. Naturality of completion, Chevalley chains, and
current envelopes gives the final assertion.

Finite simple-root windows

Let 𝐼Δ be the multiset of simple roots of the Gritsenko–Nikulin Borcherds superalgebra. A repeated
imaginary simple root is written once for each copy. The parity map is

                                                 𝑝 : 𝐼Δ −→ ℤ/2.

Choose a proper height function
                                                 ℎ : 𝐼Δ −→ ℤ>0
whose sublevel sets are finite. Put

                                         𝐼𝐻 = { 𝑖 ∈ 𝐼Δ : ℎ ( 𝑖 ) ≤ 𝐻 } .

The restriction of the invariant form gives a finite symmetric Borcherds–Cartan matrix

                                   𝐴 𝐻 = ( 𝑎𝑖𝑗 ) 𝑖,𝑗 ∈ 𝐼𝐻 ,
                                    𝑎𝑖𝑖 = 2                   for real 𝑖,
                                    𝑎𝑖𝑖 ≤ 0                   for imaginary 𝑖,
                                    𝑎𝑖𝑗 ≤ 0                   for 𝑖 ≠ 𝑗 .

The evenness of the root lattice makes every imaginary diagonal entry even.

Construction 150.1 (The Igusa Borcherds quiver). Order 𝐼𝐻 . For 𝑖 < 𝑗 place − 𝑎𝑖𝑗 arrows from 𝑖 to 𝑗 .

loops. A real vertex has loop number zero. Denote the resulting quiver by 𝑄 Δ,𝐻 .

Proposition 150.2 (Euler form). The symmetrized Euler form of 𝑄 Δ,𝐻 is 𝐴 𝐻 :

                                            ⟨𝑒 𝑖 , 𝑒𝑗 ⟩ + ⟨𝑒𝑗 , 𝑒 𝑖 ⟩ = 𝑎𝑖𝑗 .

Proof. For 𝑖 ≠ 𝑗 , the total number of arrows between the two vertices is − 𝑎𝑖𝑗 , so the symmetrized Euler entry is
𝑎𝑖𝑗 . At 𝑖 the value is 2 − 2 𝑔𝑖 = 𝑎𝑖𝑖 .

on the old vertices form an exact extension-closed subcategory. This gives compatible Hall embeddings.

Theorem 152.1 (Finite-window Hall theorem). The composition subalgebra generated by the simple
representations of 𝑄 Δ,𝐻 is the positive quantum generalized Kac–Moody algebra attached to the underlying
even Borcherds–Cartan matrix 𝐴 𝐻 . Semisimple perverse sheaves on the representation varieties give its
canonical basis. The semi-derived Hall algebra of two-periodic complexes realizes the corresponding full
ordinary quantum generalized Kac–Moody algebra after Cartan reduction.
Proof. The positive statement is the quiver-with-loops construction of Kang–Schiffmann. Their perverse-sheaf
category gives the canonical basis. Lu’s semi-derived Hall theorem realizes the full quantum generalized Kac–
Moody algebra from the same quiver with loops; the Cartan reduction identifies the two torus copies [2,49,58].

152.1     Parity and the quantized superdatum
The Gritsenko–Nikulin denominator algebra carries a parity map on its simple-root multiset. Extend
it additively to the root lattice. Hong’s construction applied to this Borcherds superdatum gives the
positive Borel
                                              𝑈𝑞+ (𝔤Δ5 ( 𝐻 ) )
and the full quantized Borcherds superalgebra 𝑈𝑞 (𝔤Δ5 ( 𝐻 ) ).

Theorem 152.2 (Parallel Hall and supergroup realizations). The quiver Hall algebra and Hong’s quantum
supergroup are two functorial realizations of the same ﬁnite Borcherds–Cartan window:
   (i) the quiver Hall algebra realizes the underlying ordinary generalized Kac–Moody presentation and
       its canonical basis;
  (ii) Hong’s algebra realizes the parity-reﬁned super presentation and its universal 𝑅-matrix;
 (iii) their root lattices, Cartan forms, height ﬁltrations, and Weyl denominators agree after applying the
       parity supertrace.
Proof. The first statement is the finite-window Hall theorem. The second is Hong’s quantized Borcherds-
superalgebra theorem. Both presentations use 𝐴 𝐻 and the same simple-root lattice. The parity map changes
ordinary dimensions into superdimensions in the PBW character. The denominator theorem identifies this signed
PBW product with the Gritsenko–Nikulin product.

The three-Calabi–Yau completion

Let 𝑘𝑄 Δ,𝐻 be the path algebra. Its completed three-Calabi–Yau completion is

                                             ΓΔ,𝐻 = Π3 ( 𝑘𝑄Δ,𝐻 ) .

Equivalently, it is the Ginzburg dg algebra of the doubled quiver with a degree −1 reverse arrow for every
arrow and a degree −2 loop at every vertex, with differential determined by the canonical potential.

Theorem 153.1 (Borcherds–Ginzburg tower). For every 𝐻 :
   (i) ΓΔ,𝐻 is a complete three-Calabi–Yau dg algebra;
  (ii) the degree-zero inclusion 𝑘𝑄 Δ,𝐻 → ΓΔ,𝐻 is functorial in full-subquiver inclusions;
 (iii) the Hall composition algebra of 𝑘𝑄Δ,𝐻 is the underlying ordinary positive quantum generalized
       Kac–Moody algebra;
 (iv) the inclusions 𝐻 ≤ 𝐻 ′ form a compatible tower of dg categories and Hall algebras.
Proof. The Calabi–Yau assertion is Keller’s theorem on deformed Calabi–Yau completions, in the Ginzburg
model [35, 50]. Full-subquiver inclusions extend to reverse arrows, loops, and differentials. The Hall statement is
the finite-window Hall theorem.

   Define the proper-height objects

                             ΓΔ = lim ΓΔ,𝐻 ,         b𝑞 (𝔤Δ ) = lim 𝑈𝑞 (𝔤Δ ( 𝐻 ) ) .

                                     𝐻                             𝐻

The first is locally three-Calabi–Yau: every finite collection of objects lies in a finite three-Calabi–Yau
window. The second is Hong’s root-height-complete quantum symmetry.

Coordinate-framed orientation on finite critical
charts

Fix 𝐻 and a dimension vector d. The representation space of the graded doubled quiver underlying ΓΔ,𝐻
is affine. Order its vertex, arrow, reverse-arrow, and degree-2 loop coordinates. The corresponding volume
line is trivial. The derived moment-map equations form a symmetric three-Calabi–Yau deformation
complex whose determinant is the square of this ordered coordinate line.

Theorem 155.1 (Multiplicative coordinate-framed orientation). A choice of order for the vertex, arrow,
reverse-arrow, and loop coordinates trivializes the ambient canonical line and supplies an orientation of
every ﬁnite-dimensional critical representation chart of ΓΔ,𝐻 . Under direct sum and the Hall extension cor-
respondence, the orientation line is the tensor product of the two diagonal orientation lines. The construction
is associative for three summands and compatible with full-subquiver inclusions.
Proof. On the extension chart, every arrow matrix is block upper triangular. The tangent complex splits into two
diagonal deformation complexes and two off-diagonal complexes exchanged by three-Calabi–Yau duality. The
paired off-diagonal part carries the standard determinant square root induced by the three-Calabi–Yau duality
pairing. The ordered diagonal coordinate lines therefore multiply. A three-block decomposition gives the same
tensor order in both parenthesizations. Full-subquiver inclusion appends an ordered set of coordinates.

Corollary 155.2 (Oriented critical chart tower). The ﬁnite critical charts carry compatible coordinate-
framed vanishing-cycle complexes. Their Grothendieck groups reﬁne the quiver Hall tower by the three-
Calabi–Yau critical coeﬃcients.
Proof. The chosen coordinate framings are compatible with direct sum, extension correspondences, and full-
subquiver inclusions in the fixed root order. Vanishing cycles are functorial for these oriented critical charts, so
their convolution complexes and Grothendieck groups form the stated tower.

Theorem 158.1 (Classical local theory and exact quantum harmonic sector). For every ﬁnite height 𝐻
the shifted-cotangent action deﬁnes a classical mixed holomorphic–topological factorization algebra on
ℝ2top × 𝐸 . Harmonic reduction on a compact product cell gives the ﬁnite-dimensional cotangent BV theory
of 𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) , and this reduced theory satisﬁes the quantum master equation. Complementary Lagrangian
boundary polarizations give the boundary enveloping algebra 𝑈 (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) ), the two-sided bar complex
𝐶∗ (𝔫Δ5 ,+ ( 𝐻 ) ), and the current envelope on 𝐸 . The proper-height inverse system is coeﬃcientwise ﬁnite and
deﬁnes a complete classical local theory together with an exact quantum harmonic sector.
Proof. The invariant cotangent pairing and the Jacobi identity give the classical master equation and factorization
descent. Harmonic reduction leaves a finite positive nilpotent Lie superalgebra. Every even adjoint endomorphism
raises proper height and is strictly upper triangular; every odd adjoint endomorphism has vanishing supertrace. The
reduced BV divergence therefore vanishes. Canonical quantization in the selected polarization gives the enveloping
algebra, and the augmentation boundary states give the two-sided bar, identified with Chevalley chains by PBW.
Each map preserves height, so the inverse system is defined coefficientwise.

160.1    Automorphic orientation
The product defines a section of the automorphic line L 5 ⊗ 𝜈5 . On the absolute-convergence chamber,
the oriented Fredholm Pfaffian is a trivialization of this line. Analytic continuation uses the automorphy
factor of 𝐷5 and therefore glues the chamberwise Fredholm models with transition character 𝜈5 .

Corollary 160.2 (Orientation character). The automorphic orientation line of the root Dirac family is
the sign local system 𝜈5 . On every real simple reﬂection it has value −1.
Proof. The Fredholm product equals 𝐷5 on the convergence chamber, and 𝐷5 transforms with automorphy
factor 𝜈5 . This transformation law is the gluing rule for the automorphic orientation line. A real simple root occurs
with multiplicity one, so reflection reverses its oriented normal line.

Theorem 161.2 (Igusa protected completion). The quintuple 𝔎Δ satisﬁes:
   (i) its one-particle Lie superalgebra is 𝔤Δ5 ;
  (ii) its ordinary Hall presentation and Hong’s parity-reﬁned quantum supergroup share the Borcherds–
       Cartan grading and signed PBW denominator;
 (iii) its chiral primitive algebra on 𝐸 is the local–wrapped current algebra of 𝔤Δ5 ;
 (iv) its raw multiparticle supercharacter is 𝑃 −1 ,where 𝑒 − 𝜌 𝑃 = DenΔ ; equivalently 𝑒 𝜌 sch Fock(𝔫Δ5 ,+ ) =

Chapter 162


Universal property

A presentation-preserving protected realization consists of an oriented root-graded theory 𝔗, a map
from each Igusa simple-root space to its primitive states, and chosen comparison maps from the Hall,
Ginzburg, current, quantum-double, and determinant functors applied to that simple-root map.

Theorem 162.1 (Universal presentation map). Every presentation-preserving protected realization carries
a unique Lie-superalgebra morphism

                                             𝔫Δ5 ,+ −→ Prim(𝔗)

extending the simple-root map. Functoriality produces compatible morphisms from the Hall algebra, the
Ginzburg completion, the local–wrapped current theory, the completed quantum group, and the virtual
root determinant of 𝔎Δ to their selected targets in 𝔗. These morphisms are equivalences when the primitive
map and the selected functorial comparison maps are equivalences.
Proof. The Borcherds generators and relations give the unique primitive Lie morphism. Hall convolution, Cal-
abi–Yau completion, chiral Chevalley chains, quantized doubling, and the diagonal Fredholm construction are
functorial in the root presentation. PBW promotes a primitive equivalence to the completed enveloping and Fock
objects. The remaining equivalences are exactly the selected comparison maps in the definition of a presentation-
preserving realization.

Chapter 163


The eight diagonal-divisor forms

Cléry and Gritsenko classify eight genus-two modular forms whose divisor is the diagonal with order
one [17]. Gritsenko–Nikulin and the CHL denominator constructions give the sourced reflective pre-
sentations used for the corresponding rows [36–38, 40–42]. Every row has a minimal virtual product
superspace obtained from its Fourier exponents, together with a Fock determinant and a diagonal Fred-
holm operator. A sourced denominator presentation refines this product model by its Borcherds–Cartan
bracket, Weyl group, and quantum supergroup. The half-integral rows are formed on their multiplier
covers.

Theorem 163.1 (Eight-row algebraic realization). Each diagonal-divisor row has a proper-height virtual
root superspace, a super-Fock determinant, a colored abelian current theory, and a trace-class Fredholm
denominator operator whose character is the corresponding automorphic product. For every row equipped
with its reﬂective denominator presentation, the ﬁnite-window Borcherds quiver, ordinary Hall category,
three-Calabi–Yau completion, and parity-reﬁned quantum supergroup reﬁne this product model and
recover the same denominator character.
Proof. Local finiteness of the product exponents gives the graded superspace and its proper-height completion.
The Fock and Fredholm constructions depend only on these signed multiplicities and reproduce the product
coefficientwise. A reflective denominator presentation supplies a symmetrizable Borcherds superdatum; the quiver,
Hall, Ginzburg, current, and Hong constructions then apply to that datum and preserve its PBW denominator.

Determinants, brackets, and Hall comparison
data

The half-index determinant, theta normalization, Borcherds denominator, reduced scalar theorem,
charge Gram map, and geometric Hall comparison require the following distinct constructions:
  (i) the automorphic square root is completed entirely within the Borcherds product theorem;
  (ii) the BKM bracket comes from the Gritsenko–Nikulin algebra;
 (iii) the bar–Chevalley comparison gives the denominator a homological source;
 (iv) the current and BF constructions give an exact first-order algebraic model;
  (v) orientation data refine determinant lines, while an operator Pfaffian requires an actual Fredholm
      family;
 (vi) Weyl reflections are attached to real roots, and imaginary roots enter through multiplicities;
 (vii) the compact Hall comparison uses additive Mukai charge, the polarized central extension, and
       hybrid local–wrapped sectors.

                                         M       ch                 ⊥

Theorem 168.3 (Four-colour differential). The interface differential squares to zero. Its codimension-
two terms are exactly the associative relations in the two colours, the two module relations, and the mixed
distributive relation.
Proof. Filter by total word length. Every component of the square contracts two adjacent edges. Disjoint contrac-
tions cancel by the determinant-line sign. Nested contractions in one colour cancel by associativity. A contraction
meeting the interface cancels by the corresponding module axiom. The remaining square has one edge of each
colour and is the aligned distributive square. The datum 𝜆 identifies its two composites.

Compatible modules and the mixed centre

A compatible module is a triple ( 𝑀 ch , 𝑀 ⊥ , 𝜅 ) with an 𝐴ch action, an 𝐴⊥ action, and an interface
comparison over 𝐾 . Let Modmix
                            𝔸 be their stable category.

Definition 169.1 (Mixed derived centre). The mixed derived centre is
                                                           
                                    𝑍 mix (𝔸) = End idModmix .      𝔸


It is computed by the endomorphisms of the diagonal compatible bimodule.

Theorem 169.2 (Universal interior action). 𝑍 mix (𝔸) carries an internal E2 algebra structure. Every
coherent interior action on the mixed boundary category factors uniquely through this centre. Morita-
equivalent complete mixed data have equivalent centres.
Proof. Endomorphisms of the identity functor form the higher centre of a module category. Composition and
insertion of natural transformations give the brace operations and hence the E2 structure. An interior operator acts
naturally on every compatible module and therefore defines an endomorphism of the identity. The factorization
is the universal property of the endomorphism object. Morita equivalence identifies the module categories and
their identity functors.

Theorem 174.1 (Graphwise factorization). Renormalized graph weights that extend to the compactiﬁed
conﬁguration spaces and satisfy the quantum master equation deﬁne a factorization algebra of quantum

Proof. Disjoint unions of configurations multiply graph weights. Stokes’ theorem on each compactified configu-

collar recovers the generating operations used to define the deformation algebra.

The Koszul–Morita square

Let 𝑃 be a dualizable boundary state for 𝐴⊥ . Its boundary dual is

                                             𝐴!𝑃 = RHom 𝐴⊥ ( 𝑃 , 𝑃 ) .

The two-sided bar sends the diagonal bimodule to a diagonal bicomodule.

Theorem 175.1 (Mixed Koszul–Morita transport). On the positive complete locus, the universal twisting
cochains identify complete mixed modules with compatible comodules over the four bar objects. Derived
tensor products of interfaces become derived cotensor products. The mixed centre and mixed Hochschild
chains are preserved.
Proof. Apply one-colour bar–cobar duality to each associative colour. The interface bar is a bimodule bar and
its filtration is finite in each total weight. The aligned distributive law identifies the two orders of transformation.
Eilenberg–Moore comparison sends tensor to cotensor. Endomorphisms of the diagonal and its trace are invariant
under the induced Morita equivalence.

Chapter 176


Planar exchange and Tannaka reconstruction

A second topological direction gives paths exchanging parallel defects. Their homotopy classes form
braid groups. A rigid meromorphic line category L with a faithful fibre functor 𝜔 has coend
                                              ∫ 𝑀 ∈L
                                    O (𝔾) =              𝜔 ( 𝑀 )∗ ⊗ 𝜔 ( 𝑀 ).

Theorem 176.1 (Quantum symmetry from exchange). The coend is a coquasitriangular bialgebra. Its
continuous dual acts on every line object. The meromorphic braiding gives the universal 𝑅-matrix on
completed matrix coeﬃcients. A spectral rational braiding yields an RTT presentation.
Proof. Tensor product in L gives the coproduct on matrix coefficients. Evaluation gives the counit. Rigidity gives
the antipode on the Hopf envelope. The braiding defines the coquasitriangular pairing, and the hexagon identities
give the Yang–Baxter equation. Writing this naturality on a generating fibre produces the RTT relation.

BRST reduction

Let 𝑄 be a square-zero derivation of a mixed datum. Extend 𝑄 factorwise to every bar word. Since 𝑄 is
a derivation for each structure map, it anticommutes with every bar differential.

Theorem 177.1 (BRST–bar bicomplex). The total differential

                                         𝐷 = 𝑄 + 𝑏ch + 𝑏⊥ + 𝑏mix

satisﬁes 𝐷 2 = 0. A bounded-below complete ﬁltration gives a convergent spectral sequence
                                                                      
                            𝐸1 = 𝐻𝑄 Barmix (𝔸; 𝐾 ) =⇒ 𝐻𝐷 Barmix (𝔸; 𝐾 ) .

When 𝑄 -cohomology is concentrated and tensor-exact, the target is the mixed bar of the reduced datum.
Proof. The square vanishes by the derivation identity and the four-colour bar theorem. The filtration by BRST
degree gives the spectral sequence. Concentration and tensor exactness identify the 𝐸1 differential with the bar
differential of the reduced operations.

                     𝑇 𝑎 = 0,      𝐺 𝜇𝜈 + Λ 𝑔 𝜇𝜈 = 0,      𝑔 𝜇𝜈 = 𝜂𝑎𝑏 𝑒 𝜇𝑎 𝑒 𝜈𝑏 .

Chapter 180


Four equivalent linear complexes

Fix a constant-curvature solution ( 𝑒, 𝜔). There are four deformation complexes.
   (i) The flat adjoint complex Ω• ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) with differential 𝑑 A .
  (ii) The Einstein–Cartan complex on ( 𝛿 𝑒, 𝛿 𝜔) with linearized torsion and curvature.
 (iii) The metric Einstein complex on ℎ 𝜇𝜈 with linearized Einstein operator.
 (iv) The Calabi complex resolving local Killing fields by the Killing, linearized curvature, and Bianchi
      operators.

Theorem 180.1 (Chain equivalence of gravitational presentations). An invertible torsion-free dreibein
gives chain maps among the four complexes. These maps are quasi-isomorphisms on every geodesically
convex open set. Their cohomology describes inﬁnitesimal isometries, physical linearized ﬁelds, and Bianchi
identities in the four languages.
Proof. The dreibein identifies internal translations with tangent vectors and internal Lorentz rotations with
skew endomorphisms. Splitting 𝑑 A in this basis gives the linearized torsion and curvature operators. Eliminating
𝛿 𝜔 by the linearized torsion equation gives the metric Einstein operator. The symbol sequence is the Calabi
symbol sequence and is exact on constant-curvature space. The Poincaré lemma for the flat adjoint connection
and exactness of the symbol homotopies give local quasi-isomorphisms.

Einstein completion of a conformal pair

Let 𝐴 𝐿 and 𝐴 𝑅 be conformal mixed boundary data with equal Brown–Henneaux central charge. Their
projective families map to the same P𝑐 ( 𝑋 ). Define their relative field algebra by the homotopy fibre
product over the projective tangent algebra.

Definition 182.1 (Einstein BV completion). The Einstein completion is

                         EinBV3 ( 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴 𝑅 ) = 𝑇 ∗ [−1] MC 𝔤grav ⋉Ham 𝔪 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴𝑅 .
                                                                                 


Here 𝔤grav is the Cartan deformation algebra and 𝔪 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴𝑅 is the relative mixed deformation algebra after
projective matching.

Theorem 182.2 (Einstein completion theorem). The completion has a canonical BV action. Its pure
gravitational sector is three-dimensional Einstein gravity. Its matter sector is the relative deformation
theory of the conformal pair. The coupling is the moment map for the projective and diffeomorphism
actions. The Bianchi identity of the full system is the 𝐿∞ identity of the semidirect product.
Proof. The homotopy fibre product matches the projective gauge fields and retains the relative matter directions.
The gravitational algebra acts on those directions by conformal transport. The semidirect product is therefore
an 𝐿∞ algebra. Shifted cotangent transgression gives the BV action and its master equation. Restriction to the
gravitational summand gives the Cartan action. The mixed term is the Hamiltonian pairing of the gauge field with
the matter moment map.

The four complexes in coordinates

184.1     Flat adjoint complex
At a flat Cartan connection A, the first complex is
                                     𝑑A                     𝑑A                      𝑑A
                     Ω0 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) −−→ Ω1 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) −−→ Ω2 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) −−→ Ω3 ( 𝑀 , 𝔤Λ ) .
Degree zero is the gauge parameter, degree one is the fluctuation, degree two is the linearized curvature,
and degree three is the Bianchi identity.

184.2     Einstein–Cartan complex
Write a gauge parameter as ( 𝜉 𝑎 , 𝜆 𝑎 ) and a fluctuation as ( 𝑢 𝑎 , 𝑣 𝑎 ) = ( 𝛿 𝑒 𝑎 , 𝛿 𝜔 𝑎 ). The first differential is
                                            𝑢 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝜉 𝑎 + 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 𝜆 𝑐 ,
                                            𝑣 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝜆 𝑎 + ℓ −2 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 𝜉 𝑐 .
The second differential is
                                          𝛿𝑇 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝑢 𝑎 + 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑣 𝑏 ∧ 𝑒 𝑐 ,
                                          𝛿 𝐹 𝑎 = 𝐷 𝜔 𝑣 𝑎 + ℓ −2 𝜖 𝑎 𝑏𝑐 𝑒 𝑏 ∧ 𝑢 𝑐 .
The third differential is the linearized pair of Cartan Bianchi identities.
Proposition 184.1 (Chain identity). The composite of the ﬁrst two Einstein–Cartan differentials vanishes
on a constant-curvature torsion-free background.
Proof. Apply 𝐷 2𝜔 to 𝜉 and 𝜆. Replace the background curvature by −ℓ −2 𝑒 ∧ 𝑒 and use 𝐷 𝜔 𝑒 = 0. The curvature
terms cancel the explicit ℓ −2 terms. The remaining expressions are Jacobi identities for 𝜖 𝑎𝑏 𝑐 .

Theorem 184.2 (Explicit metric reduction). Solving the linearized torsion equation for 𝑣 = 𝛿 𝜔 and
substituting into the linearized curvature equation gives 𝐸Λ ℎ = 0. The kernel of the map from ( 𝑢, 𝑣 ) to ℎ
is the inﬁnitesimal Lorentz action.
Proof. The torsion equation is algebraic in the antisymmetric part of the covariant derivative of 𝑢 and determines
𝑣 . The symmetric part of 𝑢 is ℎ/2. Substitution into 𝛿 𝐹 = 0 yields the standard variation of the Ricci tensor and
the displayed Einstein operator. An antisymmetric 𝑢 𝜇𝜈 is exactly a Lorentz rotation of the dreibein.

Matter stress tensor and the Einstein moment map

Let 𝔪 be the relative field algebra of a conformal pair. Projective covariance gives a Hamiltonian action
of vector fields and therefore a moment map

                                      𝜇matt : 𝔪 −→ Ω0,• ( 𝑋 , 𝐾𝑋⊗2 ) [−1] .

Its classical component is the stress tensor.

Theorem 186.1 (Conservation from the master equation). The coupled BV action

                                        𝑆tot = 𝑆grav + 𝑆matt + ⟨ℎ, 𝜇matt ⟩

satisﬁes the classical master equation precisely when the matter moment map is equivariant. The component
of that equation linear in the diffeomorphism ghost is

                                                    ∇ 𝜇𝑇𝜇𝜈 = 0.

Proof. The master bracket of the coupling with itself is the defect of equivariance. The bracket with the gravi-
tational action is the infinitesimal diffeomorphism of the moment map. Their cancellation is the Hamiltonian
equivariance identity. Pairing the identity with the diffeomorphism ghost and integrating by parts gives stress-tensor
conservation.

One-loop gravity and analytic torsion

Expand Chern–Simons theory around an acyclic flat connection A. Gauge fixing gives the Laplacians
of the flat adjoint de Rham complex.

Theorem 187.1 (One-loop determinant). The absolute value of the one-loop partition function is the
square root of the Ray–Singer torsion of the ﬂat adjoint bundle:

                                     | 𝑍 1-loop (A)| = 𝜏RS ( 𝑀 , ad A) 1/2 .

The phase is the eta-invariant contribution ﬁxed by the framing convention.
Proof. Bosonic and ghost Gaussian integrals give alternating determinants of the Laplacians in the flat de Rham
complex. Their product is the analytic torsion. The odd signature operator supplies the determinant phase. This
is the standard stationary-phase calculation for Chern–Simons theory [68, 73].

   The same determinant complex is obtained from the Einstein–Cartan, metric, and Calabi pre-
sentations through their chain equivalences. One-loop equality is therefore a consequence of quasi-
isomorphism together with compatible determinant-line orientations.

Higher-spin holonomy and boundary charges

In 𝔰𝔩 𝑁 Chern–Simons gravity, a smooth Euclidean saddle is selected by the conjugacy class of the thermal
holonomy. The invariant polynomials

                                         Tr(Hol 𝑠 ) ,         2 ≤ 𝑠 ≤ 𝑁,

fix the spin- 𝑠 charges. Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction identifies the same charges with zero modes of the
𝑊𝑁 currents.

Theorem 189.1 (Bulk–boundary charge equality). For a principal 𝔰𝔩 𝑁 connection in Drinfeld–Sokolov
gauge, the invariant polynomials of the spatial holonomy equal the classical 𝑊𝑁 zero-mode Hamiltonians.
Their Poisson brackets are the classical 𝑊𝑁 brackets.
Proof. Drinfeld–Sokolov gauge writes the connection as the principal raising operator plus lowest-weight compo-
nents. The coefficients of the characteristic polynomial are the invariant polynomials and are polynomial functions
of those components. Hamiltonian reduction gives the 𝑊𝑁 Poisson structure on the same functions.

Anomalies, Hall sources, and automorphic
              completion




                   491
Chapter 190


Four native anomaly classes

The construction carries four curvature classes.
   (i) A local BV anomaly lies in the degree-one local deformation complex.
  (ii) A projective anomaly is the curvature of the determinant line over parameter space.
 (iii) A framing anomaly is the transgression of a characteristic class to a three-manifold.
 (iv) A curved Koszul term is the arity-zero component of a curved coalgebra.

Theorem 190.1 (Curvature-killing extension). Let 𝜅 be a closed central degree-two class in the deformation
complex of a theory. Adjoining a degree-one generator 𝑢 with 𝑑𝑢 = 𝜅 produces a central extension in which
the lifted curvature vanishes. Tensoring with the inverse invertible ﬁeld theory gives the geometric realization
of the same cancellation.
Proof. The extended differential squares to zero because 𝑑𝜅 = 0 and 𝜅 is central. The new Maurer–Cartan
equation contains the compensating field 𝑢. An invertible theory with curvature −𝜅 adds the opposite class under
tensor product.




                                                      493
Chapter 191


Hall and Calabi–Yau sources

Let C be a Calabi–Yau category with a Hall moduli problem. An oriented extension correspondence
gives a Hall algebra 𝐻 + . A factorization pushforward to the boundary curve gives a chiral-convolution
algebra. Relative induction supplies the remaining colours.

Theorem 191.1 (Source-to-BV functor). A morphism from the Hall boundary datum to a complete mixed
boundary datum induces morphisms

             𝐻 + −→ 𝐴ch ,          𝑍 Hall −→ 𝑍 mix (𝔸) ,         BVEnvHall −→ BVEnvfull
                                                                                   3 (𝔸) .

Compatibility with coproduct, pairing, and braiding propagates to the quantum line category. Compatibility
with cyclic traces propagates to the quantum and modular amplitudes.
Proof. The Hall map preserves associative multiplication. Relative operadic induction extends it to the mixed
colours and interface. Natural transformation of module actions gives the centre map. Functoriality of convolution
deformation algebras and shifted cotangent gives the BV map. The remaining structures are natural under monoidal
and cyclic functors.

    For 𝑋 = 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 , Volume IV constructs the geometric BPS Borel 𝔟 𝑋 , the Igusa Borcherds Borel
𝔟Δ , and their completed Lie amalgam. Applying the enveloping functor gives the cocommutative Hopf
algebra
                                   𝐻𝑋 ⊠Δ = 𝑈b (𝔟 𝑋 )b
                                                    ∗𝑈b (𝔥Π ) 𝑈
                                                              b (𝔟Δ ) .

The two Hopf retractions preserve the geometric and automorphic sectors separately. A 𝑞 -deformed
mixed Hopf algebra is supplied by the quantum amalgam theorem when a geometric Hall coproduct
and pairing are present.

Theorem 191.2 (Amalgamated source envelope). Relative mixed induction sends 𝐻𝑋 ⊠Δ to a complete
mixed boundary datum 𝔸 𝑋 ⊠Δ . Its universal BV envelope has two canonical retractions

       BVEnvfull                full
            3 (𝔸 𝑋 ⊠Δ ) −→ BVEnv3 (𝔸 𝑋 ) ,                   BVEnvfull                full
                                                                  3 (𝔸 𝑋 ⊠Δ ) −→ BVEnv3 (𝔸Δ ) .

A primitive comparison quotient induces the diagonal BV theory in which geometric BPS primitives and
Igusa roots act as one ﬁeld. The kernel of the quotient is the complete mixed interaction ideal.
Proof. Relative induction preserves coproducts and the common Cartan action, so the amalgamated Hopf algebra
produces the amalgamated mixed datum. The Hopf retractions induce maps of mixed data. Functoriality of
the centre, convolution deformation algebra, and shifted cotangent transgression gives the two BV retractions.
A primitive comparison quotient is a quotient of mixed data, and functoriality carries its defining ideal to the
corresponding ideal in the deformation and BV complexes.

The Igusa protected insertion

Let 𝔎Δ be the Borcherds–Ginzburg protected category of Volume III. Its ordinary Hall boundary is
the quiver Hall category, its parity-refined line symmetry is the quantized Borcherds superalgebra, its
local–wrapped current object lies on the elliptic curve, and its first-order determinant is the oriented
virtual-root Pfaffian.

Theorem 192.1 (Igusa insertion into the universal envelope). The protected category 𝔎Δ deﬁnes a complete
mixed boundary datum on the elliptic curve. Put DenΔ ( 𝑍 ) = 𝐷5 (2 𝑍 ) and 𝚥 ( 𝑍 ) = 𝑍 /2. Its universal BV
envelope has primitive algebra 𝔤Δ5 , line symmetry 𝑈𝑞 (𝔤Δ5 ), and Weyl-shifted one-loop determinant DenΔ .
The doubled half-coordinate trace is −( 𝚥∗ DenΔ ) −2 = − 𝜒10−1 in the reduced enumerative normalization.
Proof. The colored Ran current algebra supplies the chiral and transverse colours. The Hall category supplies
ordered particle fusion and the interface to the three-Calabi–Yau completion. The quantum double supplies
exchange. The root Dirac family supplies the determinant. The identities follow from the Borcherds denominator
and the primitive reduced 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 theorem.

The carrier lattice

The complete theory uses the carrier set

                               ℭ = {diag, ch, ⊥, int, ex, cyc, mod, BV, Ein} .

For a subset 𝑆 ⊂ ℭ, let Str 𝑆 (𝔸) be the homotopy limit of all mapping spaces attached to incidence cells
supported in 𝑆 .

Theorem 193.1 (Carrier descent). When 𝑆 and 𝑇 form a relation-excisive pair, the square

                                       Str 𝑆 ∪𝑇 (𝔸)                Str 𝑆 (𝔸)


                                         Str𝑇 (𝔸)                 Str 𝑆 ∩𝑇 (𝔸)

is a homotopy pullback. Thus a global structure is assembled from its carrier restrictions together with their
common interface.
Proof. Relation-excision identifies the incidence category of 𝑆 ∪ 𝑇 with the pushout of the incidence categories
for 𝑆 and 𝑇 over their intersection. Homotopy limits send this pushout to the displayed pullback.

Definition 193.2 (Relation-complete carrier cover). Let 𝑈 ⊆ ℭ and let U = { 𝑆𝑖 } 𝑖 ∈ 𝐼 be a finite family
with 𝑈 = 𝑖 𝑆𝑖 . The family is relation-complete when the canonical functor from the Cech homotopy
         S
colimit
                                      hocolimI∩𝑗 ∈ 𝐽 𝑆𝑗 −→ I𝑈
                                            ∅≠ 𝐽 ⊆ 𝐼

is an equivalence. Equivalently, every incidence cell and every relation among its faces is contained in one
member of the cover, with all overlaps recorded by the intersections.

Theorem 193.3 (Power-set coherence). The assignment

                                                𝑆 ↦−→ Str 𝑆 (𝔸)

is a contravariant functor on the full power set of ℭ. For every relation-complete carrier cover U = { 𝑆𝑖 } 𝑖 ∈ 𝐼
of 𝑈 , the restriction map is an equivalence
                                                 ∼
                                     Str𝑈 (𝔸) −
                                              → holim Str∩𝑗 ∈ 𝐽 𝑆𝑗 (𝔸) .
                                                       ∅≠ 𝐽 ⊆ 𝐼

Thus every subtheory, every intersection of subtheories, and the complete theory are values of one descent
object.



                                                         499
193. The carrier lattice                                                                                       500


Proof. An inclusion 𝑆 ⊆ 𝑇 induces the full inclusion I𝑆 ⊆ I𝑇 and therefore the restriction map on homotopy
limits. Composition of inclusions gives functoriality. A relation-complete cover presents I𝑈 as the displayed
homotopy colimit of its intersection diagram. Mapping that colimit into the diagram of structure spaces converts
it into the stated homotopy limit. The resulting equivalence is natural under refinement of the carrier cover.

Definition 193.4 (Exact-support deformation complex). For 𝑆 ⊆ ℭ, let Def ≤ 𝑆 be the subcomplex
generated by incidence cells supported in 𝑆 , and set
                                                                    
                             Def =𝑆 = cofib hocolim Def ≤𝑇 −→ Def ≤ 𝑆 .
                                                   𝑇 ⊊𝑆

Thus Def =𝑆 retains the deformation cells whose minimal carrier support is exactly 𝑆 .

Theorem 193.5 (Carrier-support spectral sequence). Let Def full
                                                              𝔸 be the cellular convolution complex of the
complete incidence diagram. Filter it by the cardinality of minimal carrier support. The ﬁnite ﬁltration
gives a convergent spectral sequence
                                     M 𝑝+ 𝑞                                

                                       𝑆 ⊆ℭ

A class on the 𝑆 -summand is a deformation or curvature class whose ﬁrst complete geometric carrier is 𝑆 .
The ﬁrst differential is the signed incidence map between exact-support quotients.
Proof. The support-cardinality filtration is finite because ℭ is finite. Its 𝑝th associated-graded quotient is the
direct sum of the exact-support complexes Def =𝑆 with | 𝑆 | = 𝑝. The spectral sequence of a finite filtered complex
gives the displayed first page and converges strongly to the cohomology of the total convolution complex. The
cellular determinant orientation gives the incidence signs.




500
Chapter 194


Sixteen structural laws

The theory is governed by the following laws.
  (i) A geometric carrier determines the type of its operation.
  (ii) The diagonal stores the complete principal-part filtration.
 (iii) The interval stores ordered fusion.
 (iv) The plane stores exchange.
  (v) The circle stores trace.
 (vi) The stable curve stores modular sewing.
 (vii) The transverse, associative-chiral, and chiral-Lie bars are distinct.
(viii) Chiral PBW compares the last two on enveloping objects.
 (ix) The interface bar records the mixed distributive law.
  (x) The derived centre is the universal interior action.
 (xi) The deformation algebra is the tangent algebra of the centre-extended datum.
 (xii) Maurer–Cartan curvature is the field equation.
(xiii) The 𝐿∞ identity is the Bianchi identity.
(xiv) Shifted cotangent is the universal classical BV envelope.
 (xv) Hodge-completed derived de Rham theory is the intrinsic algebraic quantum complex of the
      oriented formal moduli problem.
(xvi) Einstein completion is projective matching followed by gravitational cotangent transgression.

Chapter 195


Final synthesis

The construction proceeds by a single chain of functors:

                           𝑍 mix             Def
                                               full        𝑇 ∗ [ −1]
                       𝔸 −−−−→ 𝑍 mix (𝔸) −−−→ 𝔩𝔸    −−−−−−→ BVEnvfull
                                                                 3 (𝔸) ,

                                         projective matching
                         BVEnvfull
                              3 (𝔸) −
                                    −−−−−−−−−−−−−→ EinBV3 ( 𝐴 𝐿 , 𝐴 𝑅 ) .
Hall and Calabi–Yau sources enter on the left. Quantum groups enter through planar exchange and the
line category. Automorphic products enter as characters of root-graded protected objects. Each arrow
preserves the structure carried by its source and adds the structure specified by its geometric target.

Low-arity mixed signs

Let 𝑥 | 𝑘 | 𝑦 be a suspended interface word. The two length-lowering contractions are

                 𝑏 𝐿 [ 𝑥 | 𝑘 | 𝑦 ] = (−1) | 𝑥 | ( 𝑥𝑘 )| 𝑦,     𝑏 𝑅 [ 𝑥 | 𝑘 | 𝑦 ] = (−1) | 𝑥 |+| 𝑘 | 𝑥 |( 𝑘𝑦 ) .

Applying the remaining contraction gives the two composites ( 𝑥𝑘 ) 𝑦 and 𝑥 ( 𝑘𝑦 ) with opposite
determinant-line signs. Their sum vanishes by the interface associativity relation. A transverse and a
chiral contraction acquire the product orientation

                                     det( 𝐸ch ⊕ 𝐸⊥ ) = det( 𝐸ch ) ⊗ det( 𝐸⊥ ) ,

so reversing their order contributes the Koszul sign and yields anticommutation.

The theorem of the whole construction

Theorem 199.1 (Platonic Calabi–Yau quantum-group theorem). For every integral Calabi–Yau
quantum-group datum 𝔇, the following diagram is deﬁned and commutes up to its canonical coherent
homotopies:
                                        FactEnvform                  boundary
                       𝐶𝐶 ∗ (C)                       Obs𝑄                            𝐴𝑏

                      moduli                          OC                                 𝑍 ord

                         (2− 𝑑 )-symp                         paired double
                     MC                      Hall
                                                       𝐻+                     𝐷 ( 𝐻 +, 𝐻 0, 𝐻 − ) .

A character functor sends the lower row to the corresponding Hall, PBW, Fock, determinant, or automor-
phic series. An open–closed complete boundary identiﬁes the middle vertical map with an equivalence. A
presentation-complete Hall comparison identiﬁes the lower right quantum group with the quantum group
of the geometric BPS sector.
Proof. The upper row is the formal-to-geometric realization and boundary restriction. The left vertical arrow is
shifted-symplectic transgression followed by the oriented extension correspondence. The middle vertical arrow is
the open–closed action. The lower row is Hall convolution followed by the paired Drinfeld double. Compatibility
uses the coefficient morphisms and their filtration identities included in the integral datum, as well as the base-
change and naturality assertions of the assembly theorem. The character statement follows from PBW and trace
functoriality. The two equivalence clauses are the defining recognition criteria for open–closed completeness and
presentation completeness.




                                                            515
Chapter 200


The universal K3 quantum algebra

Let 𝑆 be a K3 surface. The Nakajima Heisenberg correspondences, the Looijenga–Lunts–Verbitsky
action, and the local ADE Yangian correspondences act on distinct geometric towers. Their common
action algebra is defined as the complete algebra generated by their kernels inside the endomorphisms of
the direct sum of these towers.

Theorem 200.1 (K3 action-envelope theorem). The completed algebra generated by the Heisenberg,
LLV, and local ADE kernels exists as a quotient of their completed amalgamated free product over the
common Cartan and grading operators. It is initial among complete algebras receiving the three geometric
actions with their actual mixed commutators. Its Tannaka coend reconstructs the corresponding coordinate
bialgebra whenever the generated module category is rigid and carries a faithful continuous ﬁbre functor.
Proof. Complete filtered algebras admit degreewise amalgamated free products. Quotient by the kernel of the
combined action map to the endomorphism algebra of the geometric tower. This quotient has the asserted universal
property. Under rigidity and a faithful fibre functor, the coend construction gives the coordinate bialgebra and
recovers the action category.

    This object gives the phrase ‘‘K3 quantum algebra’’ a precise universal meaning. It preserves the three
established geometric sources and stores their mixed commutators instead of identifying them by rank.




                                                      517
Chapter 201


The central compact example

For 𝑋 = 𝐾 3 × 𝐸 , the universal Igusa object supplies the algebraic Hall–Borcherds target, its quan-
tum group, its local–wrapped current object, and its automorphic line. A reduced oriented compact
Hall source, when supplied, is compared with this target by the geometric recognition theorem. Thus
the formal, algebraic, analytic, and automorphic layers are closed; the compact geometric content is
concentrated in a single explicit realization datum.

Theorem 201.1 (Universal–geometric equivalence criterion). Let Geo 𝐾 3× 𝐸 be a reduced oriented 𝐾 3 × 𝐸
Hall datum with polarized Gram grading. A comparison

                                         ΦBPS : Prim( Geo 𝐾 3× 𝐸 ) −→ 𝔤Δ5 +

extends to an equivalence of completed doubles, current factorization objects, centres, and determinant lines
exactly when:
   (i) it maps the geometric simple states to the real and imaginary simple generators;
  (ii) it is surjective on primitives;
 (iii) its parity-reﬁned PBW character equals the Igusa root character;
 (iv) it intertwines the coproduct, Hopf pairing, local–wrapped actions, and orientation line.
Proof. The first condition gives a presentation morphism. The second and third give a primitive isomorphism by
minimal-height PBW recognition. The fourth transports the additional structures. Completion is exact in every
proper-height degree, hence the equivalence passes to the inverse limit.




                                                        519
Chapter 202


Coda: geometry becomes algebra and preserves its
origin

A Calabi–Yau category supplies duality. A BV theory supplies locality. A boundary supplies order. A
second transverse direction supplies exchange. A moduli stack supplies states. Extension correspondences
supply multiplication. A pairing supplies the double. A trace supplies a character. A Borcherds lift
supplies an automorphic denominator. The integrated datum preserves the source of every operation.
This preservation is the simplicity of the subject: each geometric motion has one algebraic image, and
the whole theory is their functorial composition.
